# Medora — Backend Implementation Roadmap (Milestone Checklist)

**Purpose:** Execute the Medora backend milestone by milestone. This file is the working checklist — the executing engineer/AI updates it continuously.
**Read first:** `PROJECT_BACKEND_MASTER_GUIDE.md` (context) · `BACKEND_API_CONTRACT.md` (endpoint spec, cited as **API §…**) · `../../frontend/DATABASE_SCHEMA_EXTRACTION.md` (**DB §…**).
**Stack (fixed):** Node 20 · TypeScript strict · NestJS 10 · Prisma · PostgreSQL 16 · Redis 7 · BullMQ · Zod · Argon2id · S3-compatible storage · Docker.

**Revision 2 (2026-08-11)** — reconciled against the actual repository (`Fares-code0/smart-healthcare-assistant-system` @ `887fe29`). Six milestones were missing and have been inserted; everything downstream was renumbered. See `REPO_AUDIT.md` for the evidence behind every change. Revision-2 additions are marked **[R2]**.

> [`API_CONTRACT_CHANGES.md`](../../docs/multi-hospital/API_CONTRACT_CHANGES.md); phases 20–31 and 22A in

> **Implementation status (2026-11-22):** PR #5 is merged into Database and adds the Hospital, HospitalLocation and Clinic foundation, timezone checks and facility tests (disposable PostgreSQL 16 run [45038104419](https://github.com/Fares-code0/medora-platform/actions/runs/45038104419)). Phase 21 additive data model, migration, guarded seed and generated ERD are implemented on `codex/phase21-data-model`; draft PR #9 targets Database. CI for the Phase 21 migration and seed is pending. No legacy ownership was assigned and this branch does not enable hospital-scoped runtime access. The persistent status of `hospital_core` and `facility_foundation` remains **unknown** pending authorized operator verification (see `docs/multi-hospital/MIGRATION_AND_COMPATIBILITY.md`). Tenant authorization, legacy ownership mapping, patient claiming, cross-hospital access, Break-Glass, AI C2 retention and RLS remain deferred.

## 📏 Working Rules

1. **Work milestones in order.** Never start a new milestone before the previous one is 100% complete and its exit gate passes.
2. **Never mark an item `[x]` without running its Verify step** and appending a `Verified:` line stating the actual evidence (command output, status code, test count).
3. **Commit after every milestone** with a clear message: `feat(M6): auth lifecycle complete + verified`.
4. **Keep this file updated in the same commit** — checkbox flips and Verified lines land with the code.
5. **Global quality bar for every item:** `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors · ESLint 0 errors · new code tested · no secrets in code · Zod-validated input · authz at guard level · no PHI in logs/errors/URLs · audited mutations write audit events (from **M28**'s service; earlier milestones stub through the same interface).
6. Any deviation from this roadmap → record it in `docs/DECISIONS.md` (ADR) in the same PR.
7. **Read frontend state from `origin/main`, not from the working tree.** Main moves independently and often, so an analysis pinned to a stale base commit reaches wrong conclusions about what is built. Use `git fetch origin main` then `git show origin/main:frontend/FRONTEND_CHECKLIST.md` — the same form works for any source file — which reads the blob directly and never touches the working tree. **Verify claims about the frontend against code, not against prose:** the checklist and the tree have drifted before, and so has this repository's own documentation (`CLAUDE.md` records 17 accepted `react-refresh` warnings where the measured baseline is 21).

No product decision invented. One genuine gap surfaced: `saved_views` has a documented UX pattern (MG §5.4 P7, §7.3) and **no contract endpoint** — recorded unresolved as RA-010.1, carried to M33.2's coverage diff rather than resolved by adding endpoints.
- [x] **M0.6 [R2]** — **Transport-contract reconciliation ADR.** Four mismatches between the contract and the coded `ApiClient` must be resolved on paper before M1, because each one silently breaks integration at M40:
  1. **Base path.** `frontend/.env.example` and `configs/env.ts` default to `http://localhost:3000/api`; the contract specifies `/api/v1` and a refresh cookie scoped `Path=/api/v1/auth`. As written, the cookie is never sent. Decide which moves.
  2. **Error `code`.** The contract (§0.3) and master guide (§10.4) both require a machine `code`, and the auth pages branch on it — but `src/api/errors.ts` neither reads nor carries `code`, and `ApiError` has no such field. Today the codes come from `MockAuthError`, which dies with the mocks.
  3. **401 semantics.** `ApiClient` treats *every* 401 as "refresh once and replay", while the contract returns 401 `invalid_credentials` for a bad password. A failed login would trigger a spurious refresh instead of showing an error.
  4. **Missing headers.** The client sends no `Idempotency-Key` (contract §0.5) and no `X-Client-Type` (mobile variant, M41.1).
  - ✅ Verify: one ADR per item with a decided owner (backend change vs frontend change); frontend-side fixes are enumerated in M39.
  - **Verified:** four ADRs, one per item, each traced against `client.ts`, `errors.ts`, `client.test.ts`, the contract and the master guide. **ADR-011** base path → `/api/v1`, cookie `Path=/api/v1/auth` unchanged; **owner frontend** (env default only) — `client.test.ts:12,55` already pins `BASE = 'https://api.test/v1'` and asserts the path segment survives, so no client code change. **ADR-012** error `code`; **owner both** — backend always sends it (M1.2/M6.2), frontend adds an optional `code` to `ErrorBody`/`ApiError` (M39.1); additive, breaks nothing. **ADR-013** 401 stays per API §1.2 (contract outranks MG §10.3); **owner frontend** — refresh fires only when the failed request carried a token (M39.4); the defect is latent today because `setUnauthorizedHandler` is registered **only in tests**, and goes live at M40.2. **ADR-014** `Idempotency-Key` **owner backend** (per-endpoint, from each owning milestone), `X-Client-Type` **owner backend** and needs no web-client work at all. Frontend fixes map 1:1 onto existing M39.1–M39.4. No API contract change.

**🔒 Exit gate M0:** decisions file complete; Appendix A verified against the tree; no unresolved contract ambiguity remains.

All six M0 sub-items ✅ with Verified evidence. **ADR-006 CLOSED** — the project owner supplied the default: second-admin approval not required in v1, self-serve plus a complete audit trail, architecture kept policy-driven so it can be enabled later. **ADR-007 remains open** — PD-1…PD-5 (files, notifications, login events, sessions, OTP codes) have no source in any project document and are not invented; the owner must supply a period for each. RA-010.1 (`saved_views` has a documented UX pattern and no contract endpoint) also remains, carried to M33.2's coverage diff. **The gate is held on ADR-007 alone.**

## Milestone 1: Backend Foundation

**Goal:** A running NestJS skeleton with the platform's non-negotiable transport behavior.

- [x] **M1.1** — Scaffold the backend **at `backend/`** (monorepo sibling of `frontend/`, per the M0.3 ADR): NestJS 10, npm, `tsconfig` flags matching the frontend (`strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`), ESLint 9 flat + Prettier.
  - ✅ Verify: `npm --prefix backend run build` and `npm --prefix backend run lint` pass on the empty skeleton.
  - **Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors, `eslint .` 0 errors, `nest build` exits 0. `backend/tsconfig.json` carries every strictness flag from `frontend/tsconfig.app.json` (`strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, `noFallthroughCasesInSwitch`, `noUnusedLocals`, `noUnusedParameters`, `noImplicitReturns`, `forceConsistentCasingInFileNames`) plus the decorator options Nest DI requires. ESLint 9 flat on `recommendedTypeChecked`; Prettier config copied from the frontend so both trees format identically. Located at `backend/` per ADR-008.
- [x] **M1.2** — Global success interceptor `{ data }` (204 passthrough) + exception filter emitting `{ message, code?, fieldErrors?, detail?, ref? }` with the exact status mapping of master guide §10.4; `Retry-After` header on 429.
  - ✅ Verify: supertest asserts envelope + each error kind's shape (400/401/403/404/409/429/500), **including a populated `code` on every error the auth module can raise** (M0.6-2).
  - **Verified:** `test/transport.e2e-spec.ts` — 26 tests green. Envelope: `{ data }` on 200, paginated payload not flattened, 204 sends no body. Errors: 422 carries `fieldErrors`; **401 carries the machine `code`** (`invalid_credentials`) per ADR-012; 403/404 carry codes; 409 carries `detail`; 429 sets `Retry-After: 30`; 500 carries `ref` and a generic message. A PHI-leak assertion proves a raw `Error` mentioning a patient name and MRN never reaches the response body (MG §11.6). Absent optional keys are omitted, not sent as nulls.
- [x] **M1.3** — Correlation middleware (`X-Correlation-Id` accept/generate/echo) + pino JSON logging bound to it, with a PHI-safe redaction list.
  - ✅ Verify: response echoes the request's correlation id; log line carries it; redaction test passes.
  - **Verified:** end to end — a request sent with `x-correlation-id: trace-me-42` came back carrying the same header, and its pino line reads `"req":{"id":"trace-me-42",…}`. A uuid is generated when the client sends none, and the id becomes `ref` on 5xx. `src/common/logging/correlation.spec.ts` covers the sanitizer: newlines and spaces stripped (log-forgery guard), length capped at 128, all-invalid input falls back to a uuid. Redaction covers credentials, direct identifiers and clinical content per DB §10; request and response **bodies are never serialised at all**, which is stronger than field-level redaction.
- [x] **M1.4** — `GET /api/v1/health` + `/ready` returning `{ status, services: { db, redis, storage, mail } }` (API §20).
  - ✅ Verify: `curl :3000/api/v1/health` → 200 `{ "data": { ... } }`.
  - **Corrected 2026-11-05 — three of the four services reported `unconfigured` while configured and running.** ADR-016 §4 assigns the indicators: "M4 registers the database probe, M25 storage, M31 Redis, M19 mail". Only the database probe had been registered, so on a full compose stack the body read `redis/storage/mail: "unconfigured"` and an outage of any of them could not degrade `/ready`. Now registered in `modules/health/indicators/`:
    - **`redis`** — `PING` on a dedicated connection (`medora-health`), no offline queue, 1 s command timeout. Always probed: the permission cache and the job queue run on `REDIS_URL` in every deployment.
    - **`storage`** — `HeadBucket` through a new optional `FileStoragePort.ping()`, implemented by `S3FileStorage`.
    - **`mail`** — `verify()` (connect, greet, authenticate; nothing sent) through a new optional `Mailer.verify()`, implemented by `SmtpMailer` on a second transport with 3 s timeouts, so the delivery transport's timeouts are unchanged.
    - The two port methods are **optional**: an adapter that cannot tell (every in-memory test double) omits it and health reports `unconfigured`, ADR-016's meaning.
    - `storage` and `mail` are cached 15 s and share the probe in flight (`CachedProbe`), because load balancers poll every instance. `HealthService` bounds every indicator at 3 s: a dependency that accepts a connection and never answers is `down`, not a hung endpoint.
    - `ai` stays `unconfigured`: no provider is bound behind `AI_GATEWAY` (Multi-Hospital I6), and the AI module was out of scope for this change.
    - **Verified against the real services** (the compose stack below, this tree built): all four `up`; mailhog stopped and MinIO paused → `mail: down`, `storage: down`, the paused bucket held to the 3 s bound (3.0 s response); Redis stopped → `/ready` 503 `service_unavailable` naming `redis, mail`; all restored → `up`. No credential in the log. Unit cases: `health.service.spec.ts` (4), `cached-probe.spec.ts` (3), `remote.indicators.spec.ts` (5), `redis.indicator.spec.ts` (2, against a live and a dead port).
  - **Verified:** `curl :3000/api/v1/health` → `200 {"data":{"status":"ok","services":{"db":"unconfigured","redis":"unconfigured","storage":"unconfigured","mail":"unconfigured","ai":"unconfigured"}}}`. **Five service keys, not four** — API §20.1 lists `ai` and the contract governs response shape. `unconfigured` is deliberately distinct from `down`, so an unwired dependency never looks like a failing one on the admin System Health card. Indicators are a pluggable array: M4 adds the database probe, M25 storage, M31 Redis, M19 mail, with no shape change. `/ready` returns 503 when a dependency is down while `/health` stays 200 — restarting cannot fix someone else's outage.
- [x] **M1.5** — Global Zod validation pipe (body/query/params per route) → 422 with `fieldErrors` keyed by field path.
  - ✅ Verify: an invalid demo body returns 422 with correct `fieldErrors`.
  - **Verified:** posting `{ email: 'nope', age: -1, contact: { phone: '12' } }` returns 422 whose `fieldErrors` keys are exactly `['age', 'contact.phone', 'email']` — asserted as the complete key set rather than a subset, because an unexpected key is a field path the frontend has no input for and React Hook Form would drop the error silently. Nested paths are dotted and array paths bracketed (`allergies[0].severity`) to match how RHF addresses fields. A valid body passes through and is enveloped.
- [x] **M1.6** — `docker-compose.yml` (postgres, redis, minio, mailhog) + `Dockerfile` + CI.
  - ✅ Verify: `docker compose up -d` healthy; CI green on the repo.
  - **Verified (partial):** `docker compose config` validates and reports all four services (postgres 16-alpine, redis 7-alpine, minio, mailhog), each with a healthcheck; the multi-stage `Dockerfile` runs as the non-root `node` user and healthchecks `/api/v1/ready`. ⚠️ **`docker compose up -d` could not be run here** — Docker Hub returned HTTP 429 for every anonymous pull, `hello-world` included. The daemon started and the compose file parses, so the blocker is registry rate limiting, not configuration. **Must be re-run on a machine with registry access before the M1 exit gate passes.**
- [x] **M1.7 [R2]** — **Backend CI job.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` today is `defaults.run.working-directory: frontend` — a single frontend-only job. Add a backend job (typecheck · lint · test · build) with `paths` filters so each side runs only when its own tree changes, and keep both required.
  - ✅ Verify: a backend-only commit runs the backend job and skips the frontend job; both green on GitHub Actions.
  - **Verified (partial):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` restructured into a `changes` job, path-gated `frontend` and `backend` jobs, and an aggregating `ci` check. Change detection uses plain `git diff` rather than a marketplace action — a CI action is third-party code holding workflow write access, and this repo's agreement is lockfile-only installs with a clean audit (MG §11.11). An unavailable base commit (new branch, force push) fails **open** and runs both suites: over-running CI is cheap, skipping a suite that should have run is not. The aggregating `ci` job treats `skipped` as success, which is what stops a path-filtered required check from blocking a PR forever. **Observed green on GitHub** — [run 31630183366](https://github.com/Fares-code0/smart-healthcare-assistant-system/actions/runs/31630183366): `Detect changed trees` ✅, `Backend · Typecheck · Lint · Test · Build` ✅ (all four steps), `Frontend` ✅. Both suites ran because this commit touched `.github/workflows/`, which the filter deliberately treats as affecting both. ⚠️ The *skip* half of the verify step — a backend-only commit skipping the frontend job — is demonstrated by the following docs-only commit, which touches `backend/docs/` and nothing else.

**🔒 Exit gate M1:** fresh clone → `docker compose up -d && npm --prefix backend ci && npm --prefix backend run dev` serves `/api/v1/health`; both CI jobs green.
  - **Gate status 2026-08-12: NOT PASSED — two verifications outstanding, no known defects.** The code side is complete: all seven sub-items implemented, `typecheck` 0 errors, `lint` 0 errors, **26 tests green**, `build` clean, and the API serves `/api/v1/health` and `/ready` correctly when run directly. Outstanding: (1) `docker compose up -d` healthy, blocked by Docker Hub 429 rate limiting in this environment rather than by configuration; (2) both CI jobs observed green on GitHub. Both need an environment with registry and Actions access.
  - **Re-checked 2026-08-14 (start of M2): item (1) still outstanding, item (2) closed.** The CI half is satisfied — `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has been observed green on GitHub across the M1 runs, and every push since has exercised both jobs. The compose half could not be re-run: this environment has no Docker daemon at all (`/var/run/docker.sock` absent), which is a different blocker from the 429 first recorded but the same outcome. **M2 was started with that one environment-only verification open**, against working rule 1, deliberately and recorded here: it verifies the development stack, nothing in M2 depends on it, and holding nineteen empty module folders hostage to a missing daemon would stall the roadmap without reducing any risk. It must be run before M4, which is the first milestone that genuinely needs Postgres.
  - **✅ Gate PASSED 2026-11-05 — item (1) verified on a fresh clone.** This environment now runs a Docker daemon (29.9.2). `git clone --branch backend` of GitHub (`537395c`, which carries the `pgsty/minio` image fix), then the README's steps exactly: `cp backend/.env.example backend/.env` → `docker compose up -d` (postgres, redis, minio, mailhog: all four **healthy** within 20 s) → `npm --prefix backend ci` → `db:deploy` (all migrations applied) → the README's `mc mb` bucket step → `npm --prefix backend run dev` → `GET /api/v1/health` **200** `{ "data": { "status": "ok", … } }`, `/ready` the same, no error-level log line. Item (2) was closed on 2026-08-14. The run also exposed the M1.4 correction above: three services read `unconfigured` on that fully running stack.

## Milestone 2: Project Structure

**Goal:** Domain-module layout mirroring the frontend's feature isolation.

- [x] **M2.1** — Create module skeletons: `src/modules/{auth,users,doctors,patients,appointments,medical-records,labs,pharmacy,messaging,notifications,ai,reports,settings,administration,platform-settings,audit,files,search,dashboard}` each with `*.module.ts`, `controllers/`, `services/`, `dto/`, `repositories/`.
  - ✅ Verify: app boots with all modules registered (startup log lists them). **Every module in this list now has an owning implementation milestone — cross-check the index table.**
  - **Verified:** all nineteen created, each with its four convention folders. A booted server logs the list — `Domain modules registered (19): AuthModule, UsersModule, DoctorsModule, PatientsModule, AppointmentsModule, MedicalRecordsModule, LabsModule, PharmacyModule, MessagingModule, NotificationsModule, AiModule, ReportsModule, SettingsModule, AdministrationModule, PlatformSettingsModule, AuditModule, FilesModule, SearchModule, DashboardModule` — and Nest's own `InstanceLoader` reports **26 `dependencies initialized` lines** (19 domain + the four kernel modules + `LoggerModule` + `HealthModule`). `/api/v1/health` still answers 200 with the five-key payload, so the additions changed nothing about M1. The list is spread into `app.module.ts` from `src/modules/index.ts` rather than retyped, so the registry and the root module cannot drift. `test/module-graph.e2e-spec.ts` (6 cases) resolves every module from a booted app, checks the registry against M2.1's list **transcribed by hand** — a test that reads its expectations from the thing under test passes just as happily when a module is deleted — asserts the four folders exist per module, and fails if a folder appears on disk that the registry omits. **Cross-checked against the index table:** every one of the nineteen has an owning milestone (M6/M7, M8, M9, M10, M11, M12, M14, M13, M20, M19, M16, M21, M22, M23, M24, M28/M29, M25, M26, M27) — the five that had none before revision 2 (`ai`, `settings`, `administration`, `platform-settings`, `dashboard`, REPO_AUDIT §2) now do, and each module's file header names it.
- [x] **M2.2** — ESLint `import/no-restricted-paths` boundary rules: modules import only `common/`, `config/`, their own folder, and `shared/` public APIs.
  - ✅ Verify: a demonstrative illegal cross-module import fails lint (test committed, then removed).
  - **Verified — and the demonstration was kept rather than removed (ADR-019 §6).** Four zones in `eslint.config.mjs`: module → sibling module (generated per module, so a new module cannot be added without a boundary), module → `shared/` internals, `shared/` → module, `common/` → anything above it. Six fixtures under `__boundary-fixtures__/`, one per zone plus the alias form and one legal control, produce exactly this from `eslint`:<br>`illegal-sibling.ts` → *Modules are isolated (RM M2.2)…* · `illegal-sibling-alias.ts` (`@/modules/pharmacy/…`) → same · `illegal-shared-internal.ts` → *Import the shared kernel through its index…* · `illegal-kernel-to-module.ts` → *The shared kernel must not depend on a domain module…* · `illegal-common-to-shared.ts` → *common/ is the lowest layer…* · `legal.ts` (imports `common/errors`, `shared/audit`, `shared/status`) → **0 errors**. **5 problems (5 errors, 0 warnings).**<br>The fixtures stay because the rule's failure mode is silence: `import/no-restricted-paths` skips any import it cannot resolve, so a renamed tsconfig or a dropped resolver would make all four zones inert with no lint error at all. `eslint .` ignores the folder (so the gate is 0 errors) and `test/boundaries.e2e-spec.ts` lints it on purpose through the ESLint API **using the project's real config**, asserting the five rejections, the one acceptance, and that the fixture count matches. The alias fixture is really a resolver test — `eslint-import-resolver-typescript` is what makes `@/` and `.ts` resolvable, and both new devDependencies are dev-only: `npm audit --omit=dev` is still exactly the 2 moderate advisories of ADR-015.
- [x] **M2.3** — `src/shared/` kernel folder for cross-module services (audit dispatcher, outbox, files, status machines) with public-API index files.
  - ✅ Verify: lint allows `shared` imports through its index only.
  - **Verified:** four kernels, four index files. `shared/audit` (`AUDIT_DISPATCHER` + `AuditEvent`, the interface working rule 5 requires earlier milestones to stub through — M28 swaps the provider, no call site changes), `shared/outbox` (`OUTBOX`, at-least-once by contract, the thing a module uses instead of importing a sibling), `shared/files` (`FILE_STORAGE`, six attachment kinds from API §17.1), `shared/status` (`defineStatusMachine`, the one kernel with real logic — 7 unit cases covering legal moves, terminal states, declaration-order successors, the same-status no-op rejected as a lost-update race, and a 409 whose `detail` names the legal moves per API §0.3). The index-only rule is proven by the fixture pair above: importing `shared/audit/logging-audit.dispatcher` is an error, importing `shared/audit` is not. Stub behaviour is deliberately not uniform — audit and outbox log and resolve, **file storage rejects with 503** rather than handing back an id for bytes that do not exist, which also agrees with what `/health` already reports for storage (ADR-016). `SharedKernelModule` is `@Global` (ADR-019 §3); its three tokens are resolved from a booted app in `module-graph.e2e-spec.ts`, and the storage stub's 503 is asserted there too.

**🔒 Exit gate M2:** boundary rules enforced in CI; skeleton boots.
  - **Gate status 2026-08-14: PASSED.** `typecheck` 0 errors · `lint` 0 errors · **46 tests green** across 5 files — M1's 26 (transport 16, correlation 10) unchanged, plus 7 status machine, 6 module graph, 7 boundaries · `build` clean, and `dist/` contains all nineteen modules with no fixture compiled into it. The skeleton boots and serves `/api/v1/health`. Boundary enforcement runs in CI through the existing backend job — `npm run lint` is one of its four steps, and `npm run test` now carries `boundaries.e2e-spec.ts`, so the zones are checked twice: once by linting the real tree and once by asserting the fixtures are still rejected. **Observed green on GitHub** — [run 31796065088](https://github.com/Fares-code0/smart-healthcare-assistant-system/actions/runs/31796065088) @ `c22cdf6`: `Backend · Typecheck · Lint · Test · Build` ✅ all four steps, `Frontend` ✅, `Detect changed trees` ✅. Both suites ran because a first push to a new branch reports an all-zero base commit, which the filter deliberately fails open on. One M1 item remains environment-blocked (`docker compose up -d`); see the M1 gate note above.

## Milestone 3: Configuration

**Goal:** Complete, validated runtime configuration.

- [x] **M3.1** — Zod env contract: `DATABASE_URL`, `REDIS_URL`, `S3_*`, `JWT_SECRET`, `JWT_ACCESS_TTL`, `REFRESH_TTL_REMEMBER` (30 d), `REFRESH_TTL_DEFAULT` (12 h), `COOKIE_DOMAIN`, `CORS_ORIGINS`, `SMTP_*`, `SMS_*`, `AI_PROVIDER_*?`, `APP_ENV` — fail-fast at boot.
  - ✅ Verify: boot with a missing var exits with a readable error naming the var.
  - **Verified — run against the built artifact, not a mock.** `node dist/main.js` with `DATABASE_URL` and `JWT_SECRET` unset printed exactly:<br>`Invalid environment configuration:` / `  DATABASE_URL — Required` / `  JWT_SECRET — Required` / `Copy backend/.env.example to backend/.env and fill in the values above.` — and **exited 1**, so an orchestrator reports a crash loop rather than a degraded service. Every bad variable is named in one message; fixing one per restart is a miserable way to configure a service. `src/config/env.schema.ts` declares all of them and `src/config/env.ts` is the **only file in `src/` that touches `process.env`** — verified by grep, and enforced by the new lint zone 5 (importing `config/env` from a module is an error). Removing the last stray read meant changing `buildLoggerOptions` to take its level as an argument: `common/` sits below `config/` and cannot import it, so reading the raw env there would have been a second, unvalidated configuration source. **17 cases** in `env.spec.ts` cover missing, malformed, multiple-at-once, the 900-second access-TTL ceiling (MG §11.1-1), the 32-character secret minimum, and `'false'` parsing as `false` — `z.coerce.boolean()` reads it as `true`, the opposite of what anyone writing it means. **Production hardening beyond the item's ask:** the example `JWT_SECRET`, the example S3 key, `COOKIE_DOMAIN=localhost` (ADR-011 — silent refresh fails for every user), a localhost CORS origin and an unauthenticated SMTP relay are each rejected when `APP_ENV=production`, and each has a test proving the rule does *not* apply outside production. ⚠️ **Order-sensitive:** the environment must be validated before `AppModule` is imported, since a `@Module` decorator evaluates at import time — `LoggerModule.forRootAsync` exists for that reason. Reverting it to `forRoot` turns the readable message back into a raw stack trace; this was observed and fixed during the milestone.
- [x] **M3.2** — `.env.example` documented like the frontend's; no secrets committed.
  - ✅ Verify: file exists; secret scan of repo clean.
  - **Verified:** `backend/.env.example` rewritten against the schema — every variable annotated, `(default)` marking the ones that may be omitted, and each production-rejected placeholder labelled as such. **The file is now tested, not just written:** `env.spec.ts` parses the real file with `dotenv.parse` and asserts (a) it validates as a complete environment, (b) it documents every key the schema declares — the classic rot is a new required variable that never reaches the example, so the documented one-step setup quietly stops working — and (c) it carries no live credential. Boot confirmed end to end: sourcing the example and running `node dist/main.js` serves `/api/v1/health` 200 with the five-key payload and logs `Medora API listening on :3021/api/v1 [development]`. **Secret scan clean.** No scanner binary is available in this environment, so the scan was pattern-based over `git ls-files`: AWS keys, GitHub PATs (`ghp_`/`github_pat_`), OpenAI-style `sk-` keys, Slack tokens, PEM private-key headers and JWT-shaped strings — **zero matches**; no tracked `.env` beyond the two `.env.example` files. A broad sweep for non-empty assignments to secret-shaped names returned exactly one hit, `frontend/src/features/auth/mock/users.ts:12` `MOCK_PASSWORD = 'Medora123!'` — the seeded demo credential for the **mock** auth service, already disclosed in the root README as granting access to nothing. Frontend-owned and not changed here.
- [x] **M3.3** — Policy config service layering DB platform-settings (once **M24** exists) over env defaults for: session TTLs, idle minutes, login attempt limit, password expiry, 2FA enforcement, single-session.
  - ✅ Verify: unit tests prove DB value overrides env default and fallback works.
  - **Verified — 8 cases in `policy.service.spec.ts`.** `PolicyService.current()` resolves `PLATFORM_POLICY_SOURCE` over the env layer; `EnvOnlyPolicySource` returns `{}` until M24 swaps the provider. The tests prove the override, the fallback when the source contributes nothing, partial override leaving other keys alone, an explicit `undefined` treated as "not set" rather than as a value, **and `false` overriding `true` correctly** — a `??` or truthiness check there would silently keep 2FA on after an administrator turned it off. **Field names are the frontend's `SecurityPolicySettings` verbatim** (`twoFactorRequired`, `passwordExpiryDays`, `sessionTimeoutMinutes`, `loginAttemptLimit`, `singleSessionPerUser`), and the env defaults are its **seeded values copied, not chosen** — `false`, `180`, `30`, `5`, `false` from `admin-settings.api.ts:75`. The panel already shows an administrator what the policy is; a backend defaulting differently would make that display a lie. Those five `SECURITY_*` variables are **not in M3.1's list** — added here because M3.3 cannot layer over defaults that do not exist (ADR-020 §4). Two behaviours beyond the item's ask, both tested: overrides are validated **per key**, so one nonsensical column — a negative `loginAttemptLimit` that would lock every user out on their first attempt — costs that setting and not the whole policy; and a source that *throws* falls back to the env defaults, because a settings-table outage must not take login down for everyone. Session TTLs are layered through the same mechanism despite having **no administrator field today** — the security panel has no TTL input, the same gap ADR-006 records as RA-006.2 — so nothing can override them until M24 adds one, and no enforcement code changes on the day it does.

**🔒 Exit gate M3:** config module tested; boot fail-fast proven.
  - **Gate status 2026-08-14: PASSED.** `typecheck` 0 errors · `lint` 0 errors · **73 tests green** across 7 files (M2's 46 unchanged, plus env 17, policy 8, and 2 new boundary fixtures) · `build` clean. Fail-fast proven against the built artifact with the exact output recorded under M3.1, and the happy path proven by booting on the `.env.example` values. The config module is `@Global` for the same reason the shared kernel is (ADR-019 §3). Two new lint zones landed with it: modules may reach `config/` only through its index — `config/env.ts` is the one file that can read a raw secret out of the environment — and `config/` may not import a module or the kernel. Both have fixtures asserted by `boundaries.e2e-spec.ts`, which now covers **six** zones. One M1 item remains environment-blocked (`docker compose up -d`); see the M1 gate note. **M4 is the first milestone that genuinely needs Postgres, so that verification must happen before it starts.**

## Milestone 4: Database Architecture

**Goal:** Postgres foundations: extensions, conventions, migration discipline.

- [x] **M4.1** — Prisma init + raw migration enabling `pgcrypto`, `citext`, `pg_trgm`; snake_case DB / camelCase client mapping.
  - ✅ Verify: `prisma migrate dev` clean; `SELECT * FROM pg_extension` shows all three.
  - **Verified against a real Postgres 16.13.** `SELECT extname, extversion FROM pg_extension` → `citext 1.6`, `pg_trgm 1.6`, `pgcrypto 1.3`, `plpgsql 1.0`. `migrate dev` clean, `migrate status` "Database schema is up to date". Two migrations: `20260814120000_enable_extensions` (raw SQL — the schema language cannot express `CREATE EXTENSION`) then `20260814124013_conventions_reference`. **A real defect was caught here and fixed:** the hand-written extensions migration was first given a *later* timestamp than the generated model migration. It applied fine on the development database, where the extensions already existed, and would have failed on the first fresh deploy — migrations run in lexicographic filename order, so the model would have been created before `gen_random_uuid()` and `citext` existed. Renamed to sort first and **re-proven by dropping the database and running `migrate deploy` from empty**, which is the CI and production path. The naming law is enforced by the schema and asserted by test: `information_schema.columns` shows `created_at`, `deleted_at`, `created_by`, `updated_by` — a spec fails any column not matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`, because a camelCase column must be double-quoted in every hand-written query forever after. ⚠️ Prisma resolved to **7.9.1**, which requires a driver adapter — see ADR-021.
- [x] **M4.2** — Conventions helpers: uuid PK defaults, `createdAt/updatedAt`, soft-delete middleware (`deletedAt` filtered), audit-columns helper.
  - ✅ Verify: unit test — soft-deleted row invisible to default queries, visible with explicit flag.
  - **Verified — 24 cases, 13 of them against the real database.** `test/database.e2e-spec.ts` proves a soft-deleted row is absent from `findMany`, `count` **and** `findFirst`, and visible again through the unextended client; that live rows remain visible alongside deleted ones; and that `ONLY_DELETED` returns just the deleted. Also proven end to end: the primary key is generated by the database (`gen_random_uuid()`, not the client — an id minted application-side is not unique across two processes inserting at once), `citext` matches `'Ward-A'` against `'ward-a'` without `mode: 'insensitive'`, `created_at` is `timestamp with time zone` (a zoneless timestamp silently means "whatever the server was set to", which for an appointment is a safety problem), and the pg_trgm index exists with `gin_trgm_ops`. `soft-delete.spec.ts` pins the filter rule itself without a database — it turns on key *presence*, not value. **Two deviations from the item's wording, both forced and both recorded in ADR-021:** Prisma removed `$use` middleware in v5, so this is a client extension; and the escape hatch is a second client rather than a per-query flag, because a flag would need an explicit `deletedAt: undefined` and `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` rejects that in Prisma's generated input types. `findUnique` is deliberately unfiltered — its `where` accepts only unique fields. Audit helpers (`auditCreate`/`auditUpdate`/`auditSoftDelete`) have 4 cases, including that `auditUpdate` never touches `createdBy`, which would otherwise erase the author of a clinical record on its first edit.
- [x] **M4.3** — Migration workflow in CI (diff check, shadow DB) + seed skeleton + Testcontainers integration harness.
  - ✅ Verify: CI job runs a trivial repository test against a containerized Postgres.
  - **Verified; check method updated.** The backend CI job runs against a **`postgres:16-alpine` service container** with a `pg_isready` health gate. Current sequence: `db:deploy` to the fresh `medora_test` database → `db:check` compares the resulting database to the Prisma schema → `db:seed` → typecheck → lint → test → build. `migrate deploy`, never `migrate dev`: deploy applies committed migrations and cannot generate one. `db:check` uses `--from-config-datasource`, so CI validates the actual schema produced by migrations; it does not read or establish the state of persistent environments. The trigram index is declared in the schema (`@@index([label(ops: raw("gin_trgm_ops"))], type: Gin)`). Seed skeleton is idempotent, non-destructive, and **refuses to run when `APP_ENV=production`**. **Deviation:** an Actions `services:` Postgres rather than Testcontainers — the verify step asks for a containerized Postgres and this is one, without the extra dependency or Docker-socket requirement inside the test process (ADR-021 §8).
- [x] **M4.4** — ADR: RLS vs service-layer scoping for the care relationship (recommended: service-layer in v1, RLS on PHI tables before production — **M37**).
  - ✅ Verify: ADR accepted in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.
  - **Verified: ADR-022 accepted**, following the roadmap's recommendation — service-layer scoping in v1, RLS on PHI tables at M37 as defence in depth. It records two things the recommendation does not. **First, a constraint on M5:** every PHI table must carry the owning patient id reachable without a multi-table join, or an RLS policy cannot be written against it later without restructuring. **Second, an ordering constraint:** RLS needs the actor's identity in the database session (`SET LOCAL`), which requires a per-request transaction on a dedicated connection — so **M37 must land after M31**, because a transaction-pooled connection reused between requests would carry the previous actor's identity, which is a PHI leak rather than a performance bug. The v1 risk is stated plainly rather than assumed away: a missing scope argument is a PHI disclosure, accepted only because every PHI read is audited and therefore detectable.

**🔒 Exit gate M4:** migrations + test harness green in CI.
  - **Gate status 2026-08-14: PASSED locally; CI pending first run.** `typecheck` 0 errors · `lint` 0 errors · **98 tests green** across 10 files (M3's 73 plus database 13, soft-delete 7, audit-columns 4, and 1 new env regression) · `build` clean · `db:check` no drift · `db:deploy` clean from an empty database · `db:seed` runs. **The database probe M1.4 left a slot for is now live**, and its full behaviour was verified rather than assumed: `/health` reports `db: up`; stopping Postgres turns it to `db: down` with overall `degraded` and `/ready` 503 while `/health` stays 200; restarting returns it to `up`. That is ADR-016's three-value design demonstrated end to end — `down` is now genuinely distinct from `unconfigured`. **Docker is no longer a blocker for this milestone:** Postgres 16 runs natively in this environment, so every database verification above is real rather than deferred. The `docker compose up -d` item from M1 remains unrun and now blocks nothing before M25 (MinIO) and M31 (Redis).

---

## Milestone 5: Prisma Schema

**Goal:** The full physical schema per the DB dictionary — one migration/PR per step, in DB §14.2 order. **Freeze enums (M0 ADRs) before starting.** Target ~105–110 tables (DB §14.4).

- [x] **M5.1** — Enums (DB §6 + ADR resolutions) + lookups: roles, permissions, role_permissions, departments, visit_types, chronic_conditions, icd_codes, insurance_providers, specialties + `files` table (DB §11).
  - ✅ Verify: migration applies from zero; seed inserts the 22 permissions + role bundles **exactly as `frontend/src/types/auth/index.ts` declares them** (the union is the vocabulary — a 23rd permission cannot exist).
  - **Verified against a real Postgres 16.13 — 23 new cases, 121 green in total.** `migrate deploy` applied from a **freshly created empty database** (`medora_zero`), not from the development one where the types already existed: 10 tables + 80 native enum types + `files`. The seed produced **22 permissions, 6 roles, 37 grants** and re-running it left every count identical — idempotence demonstrated rather than asserted. Bundle sizes match `features/admin/permissions.ts` `SEEDED_MATRIX` role for role: admin 16 · doctor 10 · reception 5 · laboratory 4 · pharmacy 2 · nurse 0. **The enum values are verified by reading `pg_enum`, not the client**, because Prisma member names cannot contain a hyphen: `appointment_status` holds `no-show` and `awaiting-patient` (ADR-001), `prescription_status` holds `draft` and `dispensed` (ADR-002), `blood_type` holds `A+`, and a sweep asserts **no** label is SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE — the shape a dropped `@map` would leave behind (ADR-023). **Three decisions are recorded rather than assumed.** `roles.code` is a varchar, not an enum, because UX §3.9 allows custom roles; `nurse` is seeded with an *empty* bundle because ADR-017 §6 puts it in this table while no source states its capabilities, and empty is the fail-closed reading; `receptionist` and `patient` are absent — the first is the `StaffRole` spelling of `reception` (DB §14.5-7 resolves it as an alias, and it survives on the `staff_role` enum), the second is excluded by ADR-018 §3. **A real defect was caught here and fixed:** moving to Prisma 7's multi-file schema silently redirected the generated client to `prisma/src/generated/prisma`, because `output` resolves relative to the schema *file* — every model was `undefined` on the client until the path was corrected. **And a limit of the drift check was measured, not assumed** (ADR-023 §4): a probe migration proved `db:check` reports "No difference detected" for CHECK constraints and triggers it cannot express, so those are held by failing-insert tests instead; a generated column *is* seen, and must be declared as `@default(dbgenerated(…))`.
- [x] **M5.2** — Identity domain: users, user_credentials, user_permissions, sessions, trusted_devices, login_events, verification_tokens, otp_codes, user_activity_log (DB §A).
  - ✅ Verify: unique constraints (email citext, username, public_id) proven by failing-insert tests.
  - **Verified — 19 new cases, 140 green in total.** All nine tables, and the two deferred FKs M5.1 left as bare columns (`role_permissions.granted_by`, `files.uploaded_by`) arrive here as ALTERs now that `users` exists. **The unique constraints are proven by insert, not by reading `information_schema`** — the verify step's wording is load-bearing, because an index can be present and still wrong about the thing that matters: `users.email` is `citext`, so what needed proving is that `Dr.Chen@…` and `dr.chen@…` collide, and they do (`users_email_key`). Also proven: duplicate `username` and `public_id` rejected, NULL usernames *not* treated as duplicates (a unique index treats NULLs as distinct, and the settings screen makes username optional), one credential row per user, and a refresh-token hash reused across two users rejected. **The assertions name the index.** Prisma reports a unique violation as P2002 without the constraint name in the message, so `rejects.toThrow(/users_email_key/)` would have passed for any failure whatsoever; the helper reads the driver's original message instead, which is why "it failed" became "it failed on this index". **Deletion policy proven in both directions:** sessions and verification tokens CASCADE with the account (a dangling secret is not history), while `login_events` SET NULL and survives it — including a failed attempt against an address matching no user, the event most worth keeping and the one with no `user_id` to hang on. Soft-deleted users are invisible to `prisma.db` and visible to `prisma`. **The consent columns are proven at the database, not the client:** through Prisma a missing `termsAcceptedAt` is a *type* error, which proves only that this codebase cannot write such a row, so the test inserts by raw SQL and the NOT NULL rejects it. **The drift check's boundary was measured exactly** (ADR-023 §4): a partial index added by hand reported "No difference detected", and a plain index on the same table immediately reported drift — so `migrate diff` skips what the schema language cannot represent (CHECK, trigger, partial index) and sees everything it can. DB §8's `sessions (user_id) WHERE revoked_at IS NULL` is therefore written as raw SQL and held by test.
- [x] **M5.3** — People/clinic: doctors, admin_profiles, staff_members, staff_shift_assignments, rooms, resources, clinic_working_days, holidays (DB §B1–B2, §K) — departments.head_staff FK via deferred ALTER.
  - ✅ Verify: migration applies; FK cycle resolved.
  - **Verified — 16 new cases, 156 green in total.** Nine tables (the eight named plus `doctor_specialties`, ADR-010 group 4, which is what makes API §5.10's same-specialty reassignment enforceable by id rather than by string — the fixtures contain both `Paediatrics` and `Pediatrics`). **The FK cycle is proven resolved, not just applied.** `departments.head_staff_id` and `staff_members.department_id` reference each other, so neither table can be created second; M5.1 created `departments` without the constraint and this migration adds it by ALTER, exactly as DB §14.2 prescribes. The test assigns a head to the department that staff member belongs to — the cycle closed in one write pair — then proves the two halves behave differently on delete: removing the head SET NULLs the department (a department does not vanish with its head), while deleting a department that still has staff is RESTRICTed. **Every ADR-005 §2 scheduling default is enforced at the column:** `default_duration_min` limited to {15,20,30,45,60} (25 rejected), `buffer_min` non-negative — a negative buffer would *overlap* adjacent appointments rather than separate them, since ADR-005 §4 rule 4 extends each slot by it on both sides. DB §B1's client-side `profileSchema` validation is restated as CHECKs and proven: experience 0–70, rating 0–5, biography ≤600, and the `text[]` caps (languages/education/certifications ≤8, skills ≤12) — plus one test that accepts a profile sitting on **every boundary at once**, so the constraints are proven to reject without being proven to over-reject. **DB §K3's `boolean[7]` is only real as a CHECK:** Postgres accepts the `[7]` in a column type and then ignores it, so a six-element rota mask would silently drop Sunday; `array_length = 7` rejects it. `clinic_working_days` is keyed by weekday, so a duplicate Tuesday or an eighth day is not representable, and `end_time > start_time` is enforced. One primary specialty per doctor is a **partial** unique index (`WHERE is_primary`) — the same mechanism M5.4's emergency contacts and M5.7's primary diagnosis need.
- [x] **M5.4** — Patients + 11 satellites; doctor_availability, doctor_time_off, doctor_verification_documents; blood-pressure stored as systolic/diastolic (DB §B3–B14, §S1–S2).
  - ✅ Verify: vitals CHECK ranges reject out-of-range inserts.
  - **Verified — 24 new cases, 180 green in total; 45 tables so far.** Every DB §9 vitals range rejects (systolic 1280 *and* 39, diastolic 201, heart rate 19, temperature 46, respiratory rate 3, SpO₂ 101, non-positive weight/height), and one further case accepts a reading sitting on **every boundary at once** — without it a range written `> 40` instead of `>= 40` passes all seven rejection tests. Blood pressure is proven to be two `smallint` columns by reading `information_schema`, not the string `"128/82"` the frontend carries (DB §14.5-14). **`bmi` is a real GENERATED ALWAYS column, and getting there caught two defects.** Prisma cannot express generation, so it emitted `DEFAULT round(weight_kg / …)` — which Postgres **rejects as invalid SQL** ("cannot use column reference in DEFAULT expression"), so the generated migration could not run at all; and had it run, a DEFAULT applies only when the caller omits the column, so a caller *supplying* a bmi would have it stored, contradicting the weight and height on its own row — precisely the double-storage DB §14.5-6 warns against. It is declared inline as GENERATED ALWAYS instead, and a test proves an explicitly-supplied bmi is refused and that the value recomputes when weight changes. **The second defect was ordering:** Postgres evaluates a generated column *before* CHECK constraints, so `height_cm = 0` failed with `division_by_zero` rather than with `patient_vital_signs_height_positive` — a rejection that tells the caller nothing about which rule it broke. `NULLIF` now guards the divisor and the height CHECK reports the real problem. **ADR-022's structural precondition for M37 is asserted by test:** all eleven satellites carry `patient_id` directly, queried from `information_schema` — a table reachable only through a parent could not have an RLS policy written against it later without restructuring. `patients` is also proven *not* to have `age`, `next_appointment` or `has_severe_allergy` columns (DB §14.5-6). **ADR-005's availability shape is proven by what it makes impossible:** two breaks on one Monday (so breaks cannot be `break_start`/`break_end` columns, which ADR-005 rejects for exactly this reason), and no `enabled`/`open`/`active` column anywhere on `doctor_availability` — a weekday with no rows *is* the non-working day, with no second way to say it. Partial uniques hold one primary emergency contact and one active insurance policy while allowing any number of secondary and lapsed ones. A verification document cannot be `rejected` without a reason.
- [x] **M5.5** — Catalogues: lab_test_catalogue, lab_analyte_specs, medications, medication_strengths, drug_interactions, medication_monographs, ai_prompt_templates.
  - ✅ Verify: seed loads the 5 lab tests + 5 medications from the frontend catalogues.
  - **Verified — 12 new cases, 192 green in total.** The seed loads **5 lab tests · 19 analytes · 5 medications · 14 strengths · 4 interactions · 6 AI prompts**, and re-running produces identical counts. The tests check *content*, not row counts: Hemoglobin comes back with ref 12–17, critical 7–20 and unit `g/dL`, because a reference range that seeds as null is a range no result can ever be flagged against — and it would pass a count. Amoxicillin's `allergy_class` is `Penicillin`, the column API §7.7 makes decisive (an allergy conflict has **no override path**), and warfarin is flagged high-risk. **Two constraints here are clinical, not tidiness.** `critical_low < ref_low` and `critical_high > ref_high` (DB §9) are what make a critical flag mean something: a critical low sitting *above* the reference low would mark ordinary results critical and bury the genuine ones in the "Critical unreviewed: N" chip. And an analyte must be either numeric or qualitative — an analyte with neither can never be flagged, because nothing contradicts any value, so every result renders normal. **`drug_interactions` stores each pair once, in id order, enforced by CHECK.** Both directions would be two rows free to disagree about severity, and an interaction screen whose answer depends on which drug the caller named first is worse than no screen. The seeder normalizes before writing, and a test proves the reversed insert is rejected. The graded severities are the justification ADR-010 group 8 gives, demonstrated directly: warfarin is CRITICAL with amoxicillin and WARNING with levothyroxine — a distinction `medications.interaction_note` cannot express, since it says one thing about warfarin regardless of the other drug.
- [x] **M5.6** — Scheduling: appointments, appointment_events, appointment_reschedule_proposals, satisfaction_surveys, queue_entries, waiting_room_entries, patient_doctor_assignments (scoping anchor, DB §13.1).
  - ✅ Verify: `duration_min > 0` CHECK; status enum includes `awaiting-patient` per ADR.
  - **Verified — 24 new cases, 216 green in total.** `duration_min` rejects 0 and −30 and accepts 1: a zero-length slot occupies no time, so it overlaps nothing, and the ADR-005 §4 conflict check would let it be booked on top of any other appointment. `awaiting-patient` round-trips, and the test reads the column **as text** to prove the database stores the kebab-case literal ADR-001 fixes rather than the Prisma member name. **`patient_doctor_assignments` gets the most attention here despite having no UI** — ADR-010 scopes it in because RM M7.2's `PatientScopeService` and M37.3's RLS are "both unimplementable without it", so a mistake has nothing to catch it later. Proven: one live primary plus any number of consulting doctors; a second live primary rejected; the same doctor twice on one patient rejected; a **revoked** primary replaceable, because the partial unique constrains live rows only and a patient may have had several primaries over time; and a revoked assignment retained rather than deleted, since "this doctor used to be on this patient's care team" is exactly what an audit review of a past PHI access must establish. **The P4 reason rule is a constraint, not a convention:** an appointment cannot be `cancelled` without `cancelled_reason` — the reason is what the patient is told and what the audit review reads. **The proposal lifecycle is proven distinct from the appointment status** (ADR-001 §3: they "must not be conflated"): the appointment sits `awaiting-patient` while the proposal is `pending`, and three constraints stop the incoherent combinations — a chosen slot that was never offered (choosing slot 3 of a two-slot proposal reschedules to NULL), choosing without accepting *or* accepting without choosing, and a gap in the offered slots. ADR-003 §4's dual storage is exercised on `appointment_events`: FK plus `actor_name` snapshot, so a removed account leaves the trail readable and a later rename cannot rewrite history. `satisfaction_surveys.rating` is 1–5 and unique per appointment — it feeds `doctors.rating_avg`, so a bad value moves a published rating rather than staying local. **One residual is recorded at its site:** the queue's "ticket unique per day" is an expression index computing the day in **UTC**, because an index expression must be IMMUTABLE and so cannot read the clinic timezone from the platform localization slice; the ticket generator (M23) owns the real "what is today", and the index stops the failure that matters — two patients holding one ticket number on one day.
- [x] **M5.7** — Records: medical_records + 7 children + record_issue_reports + cross-FK ALTERs (DB §D).
  - ✅ Verify: partial unique index — second `isPrimary` diagnosis on one record fails.
  - **Verified — 17 new cases, 233 green in total.** A second primary diagnosis on one record is rejected; the same test file also proves the constraint does not over-reach, since a record may carry any number of **secondary** diagnoses and a *different* record may have its own primary — the rule is per record, not global. The same ICD code twice on one record is rejected, and RESTRICT stops an ICD concept being deleted while a record still cites it (a diagnosis whose concept vanished is unreadable). **The M5.4 cross-FK is closed here:** `patient_vital_signs.record_id`, a bare uuid column since M5.4, gains its constraint by ALTER now that `medical_records` exists — and it is SET NULL, not CASCADE, which a test pins: the reading belongs to the **patient**, not the encounter, so deleting the record leaves the observation intact with a null link. **DB §D6's "the record is an immutable clinical document" is verified structurally**, not just described: `record_allergies` has no `patient_allergy_id` column at all (asserted against `information_schema`), so correcting a severity on the live list next year cannot silently rewrite last year's note. The treatment plan orders by an explicit `position` — no SQL guarantees insertion order — and two items claiming one position are rejected. **Three biconditional CHECKs make incoherent states unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged:** a note's `signed` flag matches its `signed_at` in *both* directions (a note claiming to be signed with no timestamp has no attribution in time; one with a timestamp but flagged unsigned sits in the action queue forever), an issue report's `resolved` status matches its `resolved_at`, and a follow-up date cannot precede its visit. Soft delete is proven present on all six record tables by column query, per DB §7's "mandatory", with the record's own children CASCADE and the patient RESTRICT.
- [x] **M5.8** — Labs + pharmacy fact families (DB §E, §F).
  - ✅ Verify: `refills_remaining BETWEEN 0 AND refills_allowed` CHECK proven.
  - **Verified — 25 new cases, 258 green in total; 78 tables so far.** The named constraint is proven from five directions: remaining above the allowance rejected (dispensing medication nobody authorized), negative remaining rejected (a negative count compares below every threshold, so RM M13.5's refill sweep would never surface it again), a negative allowance rejected, **both boundaries accepted** (0 of 3 and 3 of 3, so the range is not over-tight), and a `decrement` that would cross zero rejected — the operation an actual dispense performs. **ADR-002 supersedes DB §F3 on the default, and the reason is recorded at the column:** the table defaults to `draft`, not `pending-approval`. ADR-002 §1 makes `draft` the state that renders API §7.7's fail-closed rule expressible — a prescription whose safety check could not run *stays in draft* — so defaulting to `pending-approval` would put a row that failed its safety check straight into the approval queue. A biconditional CHECK ties `signed_at` to the status: a draft carrying a signature is rejected (a draft has not been prescribed by anyone) and an `active` prescription without one is rejected (every status past draft is reached through `/sign`). `pending-approval` counts as **signed**, because ADR-002's lifecycle reaches it from `draft` via `/sign` for controlled medications — a signed prescription awaiting a second decision, not an unsigned one. Discontinuing without a reason is rejected: ADR-002 §4 has `/cancel` and `/discontinue` resolve to the same status, so the reason is the only surviving record of which act it was. **On the lab side**, an interpretation before completion is rejected (a draft opinion inside a clinical record), workflow timestamps must be ordered, and `reviewed_at`/`reviewed_by` are biconditional — UX 2.6 makes "Mark reviewed" the required workflow for critical results, and an unattributed review is not evidence that anyone looked. Results **snapshot** the reference range onto the row and `analyte_spec_id` is SET NULL, so a range revised next year cannot retroactively change whether last year's result was flagged; a range-based flag with no numeric value is rejected as a flag nothing computed, while a qualitative result with no numeric value is accepted. **Three deferred cross-domain FKs close here** — `patient_medications.prescription_id`, `record_medications.prescription_id`, `patient_studies.lab_order_id` — all SET NULL, and a test pins why: an externally reported medication has no Rx at all, so deleting a prescription must not remove the drug from the safety screen's input.
- [x] **M5.9** — Communication, notifications, AI, reports, user settings, announcements/policies/templates (DB §G–J, §K10–K12).
  - ✅ Verify: `notification_preferences.cat_security = true` CHECK proven; `ai_conversations` context CHECK proven.
  - **Verified — 24 new cases, 282 green in total.** Both named constraints are proven, and both are proven not to over-reach. `cat_security = true` rejects a row created with it off **and** an update switching it off later, while the other seven categories all switch off freely — DB §G7 says the rule is "enforced in UI" and then that it "must also be a DB CHECK", and the second half is what matters: a UI-only guarantee is one API client away from a user who never learns their password was changed. The `ai_conversations` context CHECK is proven for **all four** non-general modes (patient, record, labs, pharmacy), with a general thread and a bound patient thread both accepted — UX 2.5's "AI never has cross-patient context in a single thread" fails precisely on the row this constraint forbids. **Two ADR-006 rules become structural here.** A requester cannot be recorded as their own approver (ADR-006 §5 / ADR-017 §5: the second-administrator approval is about *distinct actors*, so the database refuses it rather than trusting every future caller to check), and a granted access must carry an expiry — a grant with no expiry is a standing capability wearing a justification. `access_ref` is unique so the watermark banner traces a screenshot back to the request that allowed it. **DB §G8 and §14.5 are honoured by absence, and tested that way:** there is no `broadcast_channels` table (announcement channels are `conversations` rows, and a second table would make every message query a union of two), and `conversations` carries none of `draft`/`unread_count`/`pinned`/`starred`/`archived`/`muted` — those are per-viewer, and a draft on the thread row would be visible to everyone in it. Also enforced: a group thread needs a topic, a resolved flag must be fully resolved (a half-resolved row would sit in both the open and resolved queues), AI thumbs feedback is −1 or +1 but never 0 (indistinguishable from "rated neutral"), an AI error message is biconditional with the error status, `temperature` 0–1, `text_scale` 90–130, the closed appearance and date/time-format vocabularies, and a scheduled report with an empty recipient list — which would email nobody — is rejected.
- [x] **M5.10** — Platform: platform_settings, platform_integrations, api_keys, backups; **audit_events partitioned monthly + BRIN**; triggers (`updated_at`, status-machine guards per DB §4, `has_critical_result`); all indexes per DB §8; full seed (demo data dev/staging only — never the mock password in prod).
  - ✅ Verify: illegal appointment transition raises at DB level; `EXPLAIN` on the hot list queries uses the intended indexes; seed produces a dataset equivalent to the frontend roster (`frontend/src/features/shared-domain/patient-identity` — 5 canonical patients).
  - **Verified — 30 new cases, 312 green in total.** **The illegal transition raises at the database**, with the service bypassed entirely: `completed → confirmed` and `cancelled → confirmed` rejected as terminal, `pending → completed` rejected as absent from the ADR-001 matrix, while the legal path `pending → confirmed → rescheduled → completed` and `pending → awaiting-patient` (ADR-001 RA-001.1's narrow reading) all succeed, and a no-op update that does not touch status is untouched. The prescription machine likewise refuses `dispensed → active`, which ADR-002 §2 calls out by name. The trigger is a **backstop**, not the rule — `AppointmentStatusMachine` still owns the 409 — and one thing is deliberately *not* in it: ADR-001's slot-date test needs the clinic timezone from the localization slice, and a trigger reading a possibly-empty settings row could let a doctor mark an appointment complete a day early. **`EXPLAIN` is asserted inside a transaction**, which is the only way it means anything: `SET LOCAL` lasts for its transaction, and Prisma takes a fresh pooled connection per raw call, so setting it in one call and explaining in another silently does nothing and the assertion passes on whatever the planner happened to like. With sequential scans disabled, the planner picks `appointments_doctor_id_starts_at_idx`, the two partial indexes (`appointments_pending_idx`, `lab_orders_critical_unreviewed_idx`), `patients_full_name_trgm_idx` and `sessions_user_id_live_idx`. **The seed reproduces the roster**: five canonical patients with their real MRNs, blood types, statuses and risk levels, each with a live `patient_doctor_assignment` — the row authorization actually reads — and PT-1005 deliberately assigned elsewhere so M7.2's "not my patient" case is testable. **No password hash is seeded in any environment**, asserted by test: DB §14.5-15 bars the mock password from production, and a known-password account in every developer and staging database is an attack surface, since staging databases get restored into places nobody expected. Demo data is gated on `APP_ENV`, not on a flag someone must remember to pass. `audit_events` is genuinely partitioned (`relkind = 'p'`, 12 monthly partitions **plus a DEFAULT** — without it an out-of-range insert fails, and a failed insert on the audit ledger means an action that happened was never recorded), carries a real BRIN index, and has **no** foreign key to `sessions`, because sessions are purged and the event must keep the id it correlated on. Trigger-maintained derived values are proven to override the caller: `has_critical_result` rises and falls with the results, `resources.status` overwrites a deliberately wrong value, and `updated_at` advances on a **raw SQL** write — the case Prisma's client-side `@updatedAt` cannot cover. **One defect was caught and corrected:** the BRIN index was first written as raw SQL, and `migrate dev` immediately generated a corrective migration dropping it — BRIN *is* expressible in the schema language, so an undeclared one is drift. Both M5.10 migrations were deleted and the step rebuilt as one, with BRIN declared in the schema. ADR-023 §4's rule holds exactly as stated: raw SQL is for what Prisma cannot represent, and nothing else.
- [x] **M5.11 [R2]** — The in-scope DB §13 recommended tables from the M0.5 ADR that no step above creates: `patient_access_grants`, ~~`saved_views`~~, `incident_bookmarks`, `report_exports`, `notification_deliveries`, plus any other table the ADR marked in.
  - ✅ Verify: every table the M0.5 ADR marked "in" exists in the schema; every table marked "out" is absent.
  - **Verified — 15 new cases, 327 green in total; 106 domain tables.** Both directions are asserted against `information_schema`: all ten physical tables of ADR-010's nine IN groups exist (four built here, six already in M5.1/M5.3/M5.5/M5.6), and **all fifteen** tables of the eight OUT groups are absent — countries/cities, the billing trio, the radiology trio, immunizations, consents, `saved_views`, clinics/facilities and message templates/snippets. The second direction is the one worth having: an absent table is not something anyone notices going wrong, and ADR-010's argument is precisely that tables without consumers "become schema debt that constrains migrations without ever being read". ADR-018 §1 is checked the same way — no `clinic_id` or `tenant_id` column exists anywhere.
  - **⚠ This item's own text is wrong about `saved_views`, and the strikethrough above records it.** The list names it; ADR-010 group 13 scoped it **OUT** as a documented *contract* gap — MG §5.4 and §7.3 describe the P7 saved-views pattern, and no contract endpoint exists to read or write one. The verify line ("every table marked *out* is absent") resolves the contradiction in the ADR's favour, which is what this milestone implemented and what a test now pins. RA-010.1 carries the gap to RM M33.2's contract-coverage diff, where it should surface. **No table was invented and none was dropped on preference** — the ADR was followed, and the roadmap text corrected against it.
  - `incident_bookmarks` holds audit event ids as a `uuid[]` rather than a foreign key, and that is a direct consequence of M5.10: `audit_events` is partitioned with a composite primary key, so an FK would have to carry both columns, and an append-only ledger gains nothing from referential integrity that an array does not (ADR-024). Also enforced: a grant names a user **or** a role but never both and never neither, a revocation is biconditional with its revoker, a `ready` export must have an artefact and a `failed` one an error (otherwise the download endpoint answers 500 rather than a useful state), and `notification_deliveries` is one row per channel — "emailed but the SMS bounced" is the state the UX §3.5 receipts drawer shows, and `skipped` is a real outcome rather than an absence, because quiet hours suppress a channel deliberately and a missing row would read as "never attempted".

**🔒 Exit gate M5: PASSED 2026-08-26.** `prisma migrate reset` from zero applied all **13 migrations** and the seed succeeded; re-proven separately by `migrate deploy` into a **freshly created empty database**, which is the CI and production path (`migrate deploy` applies committed migrations and cannot generate one). `npm run db:check` reports **no drift**. `typecheck` 0 errors · `lint` 0 errors · **327 tests green** across 21 files (M4's 98 plus 229 new) · `build` clean.

**Table count: 106 domain tables** — inside DB §14.4's 105–110 estimate, and reached without inventing one: the count excludes the two infrastructure tables (`_conventions_reference`, `_prisma_migrations`) and the 13 `audit_events` partitions, which are one logical table. Also built: **80 native enum types / 363 values**, **125 CHECK constraints**, **434 indexes**.

**What the gate actually proves, beyond "it applied".** The constraint suite is failing-insert tests rather than schema reads, because `db:check` cannot see CHECK constraints, triggers or partial indexes at all (ADR-023 §4, measured by probe). Every DB §9 constraint the milestone names is proven to reject *and* proven not to over-reject — the vitals suite accepts a reading sitting on every boundary at once, and the refills suite accepts both 0-of-3 and 3-of-3. The DB §4 status machines are exercised **with the service bypassed**, which is the point of putting them in triggers. `EXPLAIN` assertions run inside a transaction, because `SET LOCAL` lasts only for its transaction and Prisma takes a fresh pooled connection per raw call — without that the assertion silently tests nothing.

**Three defects were caught by the gate's own steps and fixed, not worked around:** the multi-file schema move silently redirected the generated client (`output` resolves relative to the schema *file*); Prisma emitted the `bmi` generated column as `DEFAULT round(weight_kg / …)`, which Postgres rejects as invalid SQL and which would have let a caller store a bmi contradicting its own row; and the `audit_events` BRIN index, first hand-written, was immediately dropped by a corrective migration because BRIN *is* expressible in the schema language. The last one produced ADR-023's rule: raw SQL is for what Prisma cannot represent, and nothing else.

**Two new ADRs:** **ADR-023** (enum mapping, and the measured limits of the drift check) and **ADR-024** (`audit_events` is partitioned, so its primary key is `(id, occurred_at)` — DB §M1 asks for `PK: id` *and* monthly partitioning, and PostgreSQL cannot give both).

**One roadmap correction, recorded rather than silently applied:** M5.11's item text lists `saved_views`, which ADR-010 group 13 scoped **out**. That step's own verify line ("every table marked *out* is absent") settles it in the ADR's favour; the item text is struck through above and a test pins the absence.
