## Milestone 9: Doctor Module

**Goal:** Professional profiles + verification lifecycle + availability (API §3).

- [x] **M9.1** — `GET/PATCH /me/profile` with `profileSchema` validation; license edits ⇒ `verificationStatus='pending'` + admin notification + audit.
  - ✅ Verify: license change flips status and notifies (test).
  - **Verified end to end, through the real M9.3 workflow rather than by setting a column**: the test gets a practitioner genuinely verified (three documents approved, `POST /doctors/{id}/verify`), then changes the registration number and asserts the badge is gone and `verified_at` with it — a timestamp left beside `pending` would read as a verification that is still good.
  - **The route collision that blocked this is resolved by ADR-036.** `/me/profile` is one route — "the caller's own profile" — returning `{ account, patient, doctor }` with the halves that do not apply set to `null`, and `UsersModule` owns it because neither `PatientsModule` nor `DoctorsModule` can build all three without reaching into the other. The patient projection moved there unchanged and `test/patient-api.e2e-spec.ts` passes untouched, which is the evidence that the composition is additive. `PatientsModule` returns to a skeleton until M10.
  - **The reset compares against the stored value, not against the body.** A settings screen sends the whole document back on every save, so "the client mentioned `licenseNumber`" is true every time — and a practitioner whose verification reset when they edited their biography would learn to stop editing. Both cases are tested: an unchanged number resent alongside a biography edit keeps the badge; a changed one loses it.
  - **The reset and the edit are one statement.** "Verified against a registration number no longer on the row" is unreachable rather than unlikely.
  - **`fullName` is deliberately not a licence field.** Forcing re-verification for a name correction is how a clinic ends up with knowingly wrong names on file.
  - **The governance fields are not in the schema at all**, so a practitioner sending `verificationStatus: 'verified'` has it stripped and the column stays `PENDING` — asserted, because a doctor who could verify themselves would make the whole of M9.3 decorative. `professional_number_taken` is the 409 when a number is already another practitioner's, and it is the UNIQUE constraint rather than a pre-check that makes that correct under a race.
  - **`PATCH` is practitioners-only and `GET` is not**, which is the honest asymmetry: ADR-030 RA-030.2 leaves a patient's own profile edit undecided, so there is a document to read and no decision about writing it. An account with no `doctors` row gets 404 from the write routes.
- [x] **M9.2** — Photo upload via the files-service interface (stub storage acceptable until **M25**; same interface).
  - ✅ Verify: upload → profile returns `photoUrl`. ⚠️ **The round trip cannot pass until M25.2** — see below.
  - **Built against the port, and it answers 503 today by design.** `PUT /me/profile/photo` parses multipart, checks the type against `image/jpeg|png|webp` and the size against 5 MB **before** reaching storage (so those are the answers a client gets now rather than being masked), then calls `FILE_STORAGE.put`. `UnconfiguredFileStorage` rejects rather than resolving, for the reason its own header gives: "a `put()` that resolved without writing anything would hand the caller a file id for bytes that do not exist". This item anticipated exactly this — "stub storage acceptable until M25; same interface" — and M25.4's retro-wiring is a provider swap, not a change to this route.
  - The test asserts the 503 **and** that nothing was recorded against the doctor, so the failure is clean rather than partial. The `photoUrl` round trip is the one half of the verify step that waits on M25.2, recorded as RA-036.2 rather than left to be discovered.
  - **`photoUrl` was a storage key, not a URL — fixed (2026-10-31).** Once M25.2 bound the S3 adapter, the upload wrote `stored.key` into `doctors.photo_url` and `toView` echoed it, so `GET /me/profile` and the `PUT` response carried `profile_photo/…` — a path no `<img>` can load (the web client's `displayablePhoto` drops it and shows initials). Now: the upload writes a `files` row (`kind = 'profile_photo'`, checksum over the received bytes) and `doctors.photo_url` holds that **`files.id`**, in one transaction that also soft-deletes the replaced photo's row so M25.5's sweep collects its object. The view resolves the id on every read to a `signedDownloadUrl(key, DOWNLOAD_TTL_SECONDS, { disposition: 'inline' })`, so nothing stored ever expires. **The M25.3 scan gate applies** through `assertScanClean`: until the M30.2 scanner marks the row `clean`, `photoUrl` is `null` — including in the `PUT` response itself — rather than a 403 that would fail the whole profile. A bare key written before this change has no `files` row, was never scanned, and resolves to `null`; an absolute `https:` value passes through. No migration: the column is unchanged `TEXT`, and the lookup is by `files.id` because `files.storage_key` has no index. `self-profile.service.spec.ts` covers clean / unscanned / infected / unknown id / legacy key / signing failure; `profile-photo.e2e-spec.ts` runs the real `S3FileStorage` against a local bucket socket, asserts the SigV4 presigned shape (path-style, `X-Amz-Expires` = 60, `inline`) and fetches the URL back to the uploaded bytes. The 60-second TTL means a client must re-read the profile rather than cache the URL.
  - **Every doctor-photo consumer now resolves through one resolver (2026-10-31, follow-up).** The first pass left two consumers reading `doctors.photo_url` raw — which, once the column held a `files.id`, put an internal id on the wire as though it were a URL. An audit of every `photoUrl` / `photo_url` reference under `src/` found exactly three surfaces that carry the doctor photo: `/me/profile` (+ the `PUT /me/profile/photo` response), `GET /patient/doctors` + `/patient/doctors/:id`, and `GET /me/appointments` + `/me/appointments/:id` (the embedded doctor). `patients.photo_url` and `users.avatar_url` are different columns that no upload writes, and no admin/staff projection selects the doctor photo. The resolution logic moved out of `SelfProfileService` into **`shared/files/profile-photo-urls.ts` (`ProfilePhotoUrls`)**, exported from `FileStorageKernelModule`, and all three services call it at the service layer (repositories still return the stored value). It batches: `lookup()` resolves a whole page with **one** `files` query by primary key, so a directory page of twenty doctors is one extra statement, not twenty. Rules unchanged: live `profile_photo` + `clean` → inline signed URL for `DOWNLOAD_TTL_SECONDS` (60 s); unscanned / infected / deleted / unknown id / other kind / bare legacy key → `null`; `https:` → unchanged; signing failure → `null`, logged, never a 503. Tests: `profile-photo-urls.spec.ts` (every case + one-query batching), `patient-doctor.service.spec.ts`, `patient-appointment.service.spec.ts`, the reworked `self-profile.service.spec.ts`, and `profile-photo.e2e-spec.ts`, which now drives a patient-visible fixture doctor through clean / unscanned / infected / missing / deleted / legacy key / legacy `https` / no photo / signing failure on all four patient responses, with the clean URL fetched back to the stored bytes. `files.e2e-spec.ts`'s failing-store sweep case now counts `>= 1` and pins its own row, as its first case already did: the sweep reads the whole `files` table, and a retired profile photo from a parallel suite is a legitimate sweep candidate. **Limitation:** the signed URL is verified against the real adapter's output and a local socket that does not check signatures — not against a live S3-compatible store (RA-M25.1).
- [x] **M9.3** — Verification workflow: documents list, per-doc approve/reject, complete-verification (enabled only when all required docs approved), request-docs, reject — all P4/audited, doctor notified.
  - ✅ Verify: state-machine tests incl. the all-docs-approved gate.
  - **Verified — and the gate is on the server, which is the whole point.** This item words the rule as complete-verification being "enabled only when all required docs approved". An *enabled button* is a client-side courtesy; the refusal has to live in the service or it is a disabled control a `curl` walks past. `POST /doctors/{id}/verify` answers **409 naming the outstanding kinds**, and the test asserts the row does not move — neither `verification_status` nor `verified_at`. The gate is "approved", not "uploaded": three uploaded-but-pending documents are still refused, which is a separate case and a separate test.
  - **Approving a document takes an attestation, not a click.** DB §S1's three checklist booleans are `z.literal(true)`, so `{ authentic: true, current: true, identityMatch: false }` is a 422 and the document stays pending — a document approved with `identityMatch` false is one the reviewer has said does not belong to this person. Re-approving after a rejection clears `reject_reason`, because a stale reason beside a green tick is a contradiction that outlives whoever could explain it.
  - **Required means three of four kinds.** `medical_license`, `degree_certificate`, `national_id`. `other` is deliberately not required — it is the catch-all for supporting material, and requiring it would gate verification on a document nobody asked for. `national_id` is required because identity is what every other document's `check_identity_match` is matched *against*.
  - **P4 throughout, and the doctor is told.** Reject and request-documents take a mandatory 3–300 character reason (an optional reason is an optional audit trail), every decision writes an audit event carrying it, and the doctor learns of it two ways: an entry of kind `security` in their own activity feed, and an outbox message. Notification goes through `shared/outbox` rather than by calling the notifications module — zone 1 forbids that, and a direct call would tie the notification's fate to this request's.
  - **A rejection clears `verified_at`.** A stale verification timestamp beside `rejected` is a contradiction, and clearing it is what stops a later reader inferring a verification that was withdrawn.
- [x] **M9.4** — Availability: `GET/PUT /me/availability` (weekly grid, breaks, defaultDurationMin ∈ {15,20,30,45,60}, buffer) + vacation mode with affected-appointments preview.
  - ✅ Verify: grid round-trip; overlapping ranges rejected 422.
  - **Verified, and the overlap rule is the interesting half.** `@@unique([doctorId, weekday, startTime])` stops two rows starting at the same minute and **nothing else**: 09:00–13:00 beside 12:00–17:00 satisfies the constraint perfectly and describes a day that books a patient into noon twice. Postgres could express that as an exclusion constraint over a range type; the schema does not, so the rule is in the service and three cases prove it — overlapping windows, overlapping breaks, and a break outside its working window, each a 422 naming the row. Windows that *touch* (09:00–12:00, 12:00–17:00) are accepted: that is a lunch boundary, not a double booking.
  - **`PUT` replaces, never merges.** All seven days are required. "The days you did not mention keep what they had" silently preserves a day the doctor believes they cleared — the same reasoning M7.4's grid uses, and asserted the same way.
  - **`HH:MM`, never a timestamp.** The grid is recurring civil time and time off is a specific interval; DB §S2 keeps them in two tables for that reason and this keeps them in two shapes. Serialising a weekday range as an instant attaches a date to something that has none, and the client's timezone then shifts a clinic's opening hours. A time carrying seconds is a 422 rather than silently truncated, because the column is `time(0)` and a client sending them will be surprised when they come back stripped.
  - **Time off reports and moves nothing.** `POST /me/time-off` returns `{ timeOff, impacted }`, and the test drives the mode that *sounds* like an instruction — `reschedule-existing` — then asserts the appointment's status and start time are untouched. ADR-005 RA-005.2 reads that mode as a pre-selection for the resolution screen, never as automatic action, and §7's blast-radius confirmation is mandatory. M11.5 owns the other half. The impact list carries `patients.public_id` and never a name.
  - **Authorization is `doctors.user_id`, not the platform role.** `@Roles('doctor')` would have expressed the contract's "doctor" marking and been subtly wrong: the question is "is this account a practitioner", the `doctors` row answers it, and the two can disagree — a practitioner moved to `reception` keeps their row and their patients. An account with no row gets **404**, because the resource genuinely does not exist for them; 403 would say "you may not see your own availability", which is untrue.
- [x] **M9.5** — Admin doctor list/detail with operational stats (patientsCount, appointments30d, rating, lastActiveAt) + suspend/reactivate with consequence count.
  - ✅ Verify: suspend response includes `affectedAppointments`. ⚠️ **No frontend consumer exists** — ADM-02/ADM-03 (`/admin/doctors`) are unbuilt (M39.6). Verify by contract test, not by UI.
  - **Verified by contract test, exactly as this item asks.** `affectedAppointments` is on both §3.5 (so the confirmation dialog can be drawn before anything is pressed) and §3.7's response (so the client can reconcile what it showed with what the decision touched), and it is counted **before** the write — after it the number is the same today, but a later change that cascaded onto appointments would silently report zero.
  - **Two capabilities, honoured per route.** The roster and the suspension lever are `doctors.manage`; the verification decisions are `doctors.verify`. They are separate cells in the matrix precisely so a clinic can let an office manager run the roster without letting them attest that a licence is genuine, and a class-level `@Can` would have had to be the weaker of the two. A doctor holds neither, so a practitioner cannot verify themselves — asserted.
  - **Suspension is `practice_status`, not the account.** The test signs the suspended doctor in successfully and then finds them absent from `GET /patient/doctors`. That is the whole distinction: a clinic must be able to take a practitioner out of the patient-facing directory without cutting off their access to their own records. Closing the account is `PATCH /admin/users/{id}` (M8.2) — a different decision with a different consequence.
  - **Two projections, side by side, neither a filter of the other.** `admin-doctor.dto.ts` carries the licence number and the personal contact fields that `patient-doctor.dto.ts` exists to keep away from patients, and `FORBIDDEN_DOCTOR_FIELDS` names exactly what separates them. The personal contact fields are on the **detail** only: a roster page renders dozens of rows and needs none of them, and putting them there would spread staff contact details across every list response and every log line that captures one.
  - **The statistics are batched.** `patientsCount` and `appointments30d` come from two `groupBy` queries per page rather than two counts per row — a 100-row page would otherwise be 200 round trips, which is the N+1 M32.3 exists to find.

**🔒 Exit gate M9:** ✅ **met.** The verification trail is fully audited and the API §3 conformance tests are green — 58 cases across `doctor-availability.e2e-spec.ts`, `doctor-administration.e2e-spec.ts` and `doctor-profile.e2e-spec.ts`, every one driving a booted application against a seeded Postgres. All nine routes of §3.1–3.9 exist and carry a rule that `authz-matrix.e2e-spec.ts` checks. One half of one verify step waits on another milestone and is recorded rather than waved through: the photo round trip needs M25.2's storage adapter (RA-036.2).

> ### The `/me/profile` collision, and how it was resolved
>
> API §3.1 assigns `GET /me/profile` to the **doctor** profile document.
> `PatientProfileController` already owned that path (ADR-030,
> `PATIENT_MOBILE_API_CONTRACT.md` §2.1). Two controllers cannot both claim
> one path: Nest registers both and Express serves whichever module loaded
> first, and `DoctorsModule` precedes `PatientsModule` in the registry — so
> adding the doctor route naively would have **silently shadowed the patient
> app's profile endpoint**. An outage delivered as a feature, with no
> compiler error and no failing test in the module that caused it.
>
> **ADR-036 settles it.** They are not two routes that collided; they are one
> route. "My profile" is an identity question and the answer is
> `{ account, patient, doctor }`, each satellite `null` when it does not
> apply. `UsersModule` owns it — `/me/…` is the account surface it already
> holds, and neither `PatientsModule` nor `DoctorsModule` can build all three
> halves without reaching into the other (M2.2 zone 1). The patient
> projection and its clinical enum maps moved across unchanged;
> `PatientsModule` is a skeleton again until M10 builds the care-scoped
> clinician surface, which is a different authorization model and was never
> going to share a controller with a self-service route.
>
> The ADR also makes something explicit that was already true in practice:
> **M2.2's zones govern imports between modules, not which tables a
> repository may read.** `AdminDoctorRepository` counts `appointments` and
> `patients`; `AvailabilityRepository` reads `appointments`. What matters is
> that each projection is written out deliberately in the module that serves
> it, which is why `doctors` now has three of them and none is a filtered
> copy of another.
>
> **One cross-tree consequence, caught by CI.**
> `patient-app/src/test/parity.test.ts` reads the backend controller **by
> file path**, to prove the mobile client never names a route the backend
> does not serve — so moving the file turned that read into an `ENOENT` and
> the patient-app suite went red while the backend suite stayed green. The
> spec working as intended, not a defect in it.
>
> Worth adding to the two cross-tree exceptions the quality gates already
> list: a **backend refactor** can break the patient-app suite even when it
> touches none of the paths in CI's filter, because the parity spec resolves
> backend source by path rather than by import. The fix was one path, and
> what the test asserts is unchanged — the route never moved, only the file
> behind it.

---

## Milestone 10: Patient Module

**Goal:** Patient master data + profile bundle (API §4).

- [x] **M10.1** — `GET /patients`: pagination, identifier auto-detect search (name/PT-id/phone/MRN + scope override), all filters, sort whitelist.
  - ✅ Verify: searching a phone number and an MRN both hit via trigram indexes (EXPLAIN checked). The EXPLAIN half is `schema-platform.e2e-spec.ts`'s existing trigram assertion; the **routing** half is the one that had a real bug in it and is asserted here.
  - **The detection order is the whole function, and it was wrong first.** The three numeric patterns overlap: `PT-2009` matches the MRN pattern, and an MRN like `4411-27` matches the phone pattern — seven characters of digits and a dash. With phone tested before MRN, a clinician typing the number on a patient's wristband searched `patients.phone`, found nothing, and got "no results" for someone standing in front of them. MRN is now tested first, and the spec walks a real patient's handle, MRN, name fragment and phone fragment through one loop.
  - **`scope` overrides the guess rather than being required.** Auto-detection is what a clinician expects; the override is for when it guesses wrong. Asserted the way an override should be — `?q=Scoped&scope=mrn` finds *nothing*, because the term is a name and the caller said to read it as an MRN.
  - **`sort` is a whitelist, and that is a security property, not tidiness.** An open sort orders by a column the projection deliberately does not return — on this table that includes `medical_notes`, and ordering by a column is a slow, reliable oracle over its contents. `?sort=medicalNotes:asc` is a 422.
  - **The age filter is expressed as a date-of-birth range**, because age is derived and a range is what an index can serve. `ageMin` is the *older* bound and so becomes the *earlier* birth date; getting that backwards returns the exact complement of what was asked, silently, which is why both directions are tested.
- [x] **M10.2** — Create (AddPatientDialog contract, generates `PT-…` + MRN), update, soft deactivate.
  - ✅ Verify: created patient matches the `Patient` DTO exactly (contract test).
  - **The `PT-…` handle comes from a sequence; the MRN does not, and that is the one design decision here worth reading.** An administrator sees the MRN masked to its last four digits (`maskMrn`, UX §1.5.6). The `PT-…` handle is *never* masked — every subsequent request is keyed by it. So an MRN derivable from the handle would make the mask protect nothing: anyone holding the handle could compute the digits it hides. The MRN is six random digits with the UNIQUE index as the guarantee and a bounded retry as the recovery, and the spec asserts the two are unrelated.
  - Migration `20260911100000_m10_patient_identity_sequence` adds the sequence, starting at 2000 so the seeded `PT-1001`…`PT-1005` cannot collide with a live registration on a seeded database.
  - **Registration creates the care relationship, in the same transaction.** `PatientScopeService` decides access from `patient_doctor_assignments`, so a chart created without one is a chart the clinician who just registered it cannot open. Doing it in the transaction makes that state unreachable rather than a race, and the spec reads the `role: 'primary'` row back.
  - **An administrator is refused with 403.** Registration has nobody to assign the chart to without a `doctors` row. Writing the test for this exposed that the suite's own admin fixture was a doctor wearing an admin role — it had been registered through the doctor branch to obtain a bootstrap token — so the fixture now drops the `doctors` row, which is what a real administrator looks like.
  - **Deactivation is soft and the spec proves the row survives.** API §4: "patient deletion does not exist". `DELETE` is still the verb, because it is what a client's REST vocabulary offers for "remove this from my list"; the server's job is to make it mean the safe thing.
  - **Chronic conditions come from the canonical vocabulary.** ADR-004 makes `chronic_conditions` the single source, and a free-text condition cannot be matched by the safety screen or by the `condition` filter — so an unknown name is a 422 naming it rather than an orphan string in the database.
- [x] **M10.3** — Profile bundle + per-tab endpoints (overview timeline, history, vitals, medications, allergies, emergency contacts, insurance, documents, notes, studies).
  - ✅ Verify: each tab endpoint's shape validated against `profile-types.ts` schemas.
  - **Built: the bundle and nine tabs.** `GET /patients/{id}/profile` (§4.4) is the header, the latest vitals, the active medications, the chronic conditions, the emergency contacts and the active clinical alerts, in five parallel queries. `PatientTabsController` adds history, emergency contacts, notes, vitals, medications, documents, studies (grouped by category, as the contract asks) and the patient's appointment history, plus the child CRUD of §4.6.
  - **One exception, named rather than glossed: insurance has no endpoint.** `patient_insurance` carries a policy number that DB §10 classes PII and a `provider_id` FK into a providers vocabulary no module seeds yet. A read route would ship an identifier nobody has decided the masking rule for, and a write route would need a provider list to validate against. It belongs with M10.7's governance work, where the PII rules are already the subject.
  - **Why these are separate endpoints is a PHI argument, not a payload-size one.** API §4.5 calls them a "lazy per-tab fetch"; the consequence is that each tab read writes **its own audit event**. One bundle carrying every tab would make every chart open an access to every clinical note on it, and the trail could no longer distinguish "looked at the summary" from "read the psychiatric history".
  - **Care scoping is re-proved on the second controller.** A doctor with no care relationship is refused all eight feeds with the 404 a missing chart produces — asserted in one loop, because a second controller is a second chance to get the rule wrong.
  - Blood pressure stays **two fields**, per API §4.6's "systolic/diastolic split": `"120/80"` on the wire is a value nobody can chart, compare or flag against a reference range. `bmi` is the database's generated column — the spec posts a `bmi` in the body, and asserts Postgres's answer comes back instead.
  - **A signed note is frozen.** The edit and the signature are both `updateMany` with `signed: false` in the **predicate**, not a read-then-write, so a signature landing between a check and a write cannot let an edit through after the attestation. Editing a signed note is **409 `invalid_status_transition`**, not 404 — the caller has already proved they may reach the chart, so hiding the note buys nothing and "this note is signed" is what the client should render. Signing twice is refused rather than silently re-stamping the timestamp, which the spec asserts by comparing the stored `signed_at`.
- [x] **M10.4** — Allergy CRUD feeding the safety screen (allergen, severity, reaction, notes; recordedBy attribution).
  - ✅ Verify: adding a severe allergy flips `hasSevereAllergy` on 4.3/4.4 responses.
  - **Verified on both responses**, because the fixed allergy strip is drawn from whichever the screen happened to load and a flag that was right on one would be a safety failure on the other.
  - **A second row for one allergen is a 409.** `@@unique([patientId, allergen])` is the guarantee; without it a chart could say both "severe" and "mild" about the same substance, which is the worst possible answer for the prescription safety screen to have to choose between.
  - **Removal is soft**, and it is audited as `patient.allergy_remove`. Removing an allergy *narrows* what the safety screen will block, which makes it the single write on this surface most worth being able to reconstruct later.
  - **This is where a real soft-delete gap surfaced.** The extension rewrites the **top-level** `where` of an operation and nothing else, so the allergy list pulled through the patient projection's `select` came back **unfiltered** — a withdrawn allergy kept appearing on the chart *and in the derived `hasSevereAllergy` flag*, a safety-strip value computed from a row a clinician had deliberately removed. The nested selection now states `where: { deletedAt: null }`, and `shared/database/soft-delete.ts` documents the limitation with this case as the example, because the next module to embed a soft-deletable relation will hit it too.
- [x] **M10.5** — Clinical alerts CRUD (severity critical/warning, active flag).
  - ✅ Verify: active alerts appear in the profile bundle.
  - **Verified, and resolving one does not delete it.** `active: false` is the resolution; `?includeResolved=true` reads the history. A banner a clinician cannot dismiss (DB §B12) must never be drawn from a resolved alert, so active-only is the default rather than an opt-in.
  - `message` is capped at the column's 300 characters. An alert has to be readable at a glance; a paragraph in that slot is a banner nobody reads.
- [x] **M10.6** — Derived fields server-side: age, hasSevereAllergy, nextAppointment, lastVisit.
  - ✅ Verify: unit tests on the derivation queries.
  - **Verified over HTTP rather than as unit tests, deliberately** — three of the four are derivations *from a query*, and a unit test over a stubbed repository would assert the mapping while leaving the query unexercised. `nextAppointment` is proved by writing two future appointments out of order and asserting the *nearer* one comes back.
  - `age` and `hasSevereAllergy` are computed on every read and never stored: a cached age disagrees with the birth date within a year, and a cached severe-allergy flag disagrees with the allergy list the moment one is added — which for that particular field is a patient-safety failure, not a stale cache.
  - `lastVisit` is the deliberate exception, read from the denormalised `patients.last_visit_at` the schema keeps precisely because the list sorts on it and the live answer is a lateral over four fact tables.
  - `nextAppointment` is one `groupBy` for the whole page (`MIN(starts_at)` over live statuses from now on) rather than a query per row, and the masked branch skips it entirely — a query for a field nobody will see is a query worth not making.
- [ ] **M10.7 [R2]** — Admin patient governance (API §4.11): masked list/detail, unlock, reset-credentials (secure reset link — admin never sets a password), deactivate (P4), access-grant list/revoke/approve.
  - ✅ Verify: admin list returns masked identifiers by default; reset-credentials never returns or accepts a password. ⚠️ No frontend consumer — ADM-04 is unbuilt (M39.6).
  - **🚫 BLOCKED (assessed 2026-10-24) — on two decisions, not on engineering.** ADR-030 (accepted) made patient accounts real and RA-030.4 named this governance as implementable once it was. Two things still stop it being built *as specified*:
    1. **No patient account is linked to a patient record, by design.** A patient registration creates an account and nothing else (`registration.service.ts`, "What a patient registration creates"); the link is RA-030.1's claim flow, which ADR-030 deferred with identity proofing unresolved. The contract addresses every control through the record — `POST /admin/patients/{id}/unlock`, `/reset-credentials`, `/deactivate` — so each would find no account for every patient today. Addressing them by account instead (`usr_…`) is a different API from the contract's; choosing it is the owner's call. *(The account-side mechanics already exist and would be reused, not rebuilt: lockout lives in `user_credentials.failed_attempts / locked_until`, the reset link is `PasswordRecoveryService`, and soft deactivation is `PATCH /admin/users/{id}`, M8.2.)*
    2. **"Approve pending access requests" has no model.** `patient_access_grants` (ADR-010 group 2) has no pending state, nothing writes it, and no authorization path reads it — `PatientScopeService` scopes by `patient_doctor_assignments` alone. A list/revoke surface over that table would govern grants that grant nothing. Making grants effective is an authorization design (and overlaps Multi-Hospital 22A); revoking a care **assignment** instead would be a clinical decision taken from an admin screen.
    - The masked directory half is already served: `GET /patients` answers an administrator the masked projection (M10.1), and `POST /patients/{id}/reveal` is the audited elevation (M7.3).

**🔒 Exit gate M10:** **not yet met.** The API half is built and green — 39 cases across `patients-clinical.e2e-spec.ts` and `patients-tabs.e2e-spec.ts`, every clinical path through `PatientScopeService`, and the masked/clear split asserted against the serialized body rather than against the type. The manual smoke cannot run: the frontend's patients feature is still on its mock seam (M40.1), so there is nothing to render from the API yet. Still open, and each is held on an owner decision rather than on code: insurance (§4.5, see M10.3 — the policy-number masking rule) and the admin governance surface (§4.11, M10.7 — `DECISION_BRIEF_2026-10-30.md`). ~~Documents upload and download (§4.7, behind M25)~~ — **built** (D-04/D-04b: `POST /patients/{id}/documents` links an upload from `POST /files`, `GET …/documents/{documentId}/download` signs a URL behind the scan gate); re-run 2026-11-05, `patient-documents.e2e-spec.ts` 15/15. The reveal (§4.9) shipped with M7.3 on 2026-10-23. ~~The CSV export (§4.10)~~ shipped 2026-11-05 — see the note below the M10 status block.

> ### M10 status — the clinician surface is live, care-scoped and audited
>
> **What is built.** The list with identifier auto-detect and every filter
> (§4.1), registration (§4.2), the detail and the profile bundle with their
> derived fields (§4.3, §4.4), allergy and clinical-alert CRUD (§4.5–4.6 in
> part), the update (§4.8) and soft deactivation.
>
> **M7.2's standing obligation is kept by construction.** That item closed
> with "the clause *all patient-child access routes through it* cannot yet be
> violated — there are no patient-child routes — so it transfers as a
> standing obligation to M9–M14". This module is the first to inherit it, and
> every single-record path enters through one private helper that calls
> `assertCanReach` before a row is read. The **list** cannot use it — one
> query per patient — so the scope is a join with the same predicate, and the
> spec asserts the two *agree*: a patient absent from a doctor's list is a
> patient whose chart answers 404. A disagreement between them is a
> disclosure on one side or a lockout on the other, and nothing else would
> catch it.
>
> **Masking is structural, not a convention.** An administrator holding
> `patients.view` gets `MaskedIdentity`, which has no field that can hold a
> clear value — so a handler cannot leak an identifier by forgetting to mask
> one. The branch is made once, from the principal, at the entry to each
> read; a `masked: boolean` threaded through the mappers would put the
> disclosure one forgotten branch away. The tests search the serialized body
> for the actual name and MRN rather than trusting the shape.
>
> **Holding a capability is still not a care relationship.** An administrator
> is refused the profile bundle with the same 404 a stranger's chart
> produces, which is reconciliation §5 rule 5 working as written. The audited
> reveal of M7.3 is the path for an administrator who needs an identifier,
> and it is still waiting on M28's durable write for the atomicity that is
> the endpoint's whole point.

> ### Clinical list exports — built 2026-11-05 (API §4.10, §6.10, §7.10, §8.11)
>
> The five export routes the contract names and nothing served: `GET /patients/export`,
> `GET /medical-records/export`, `GET /medical-records/{id}/export`, `GET /prescriptions/export`
> and `GET /lab-orders/export`. Each reuses its list's schema without paging and **its list's
> repository call with its list's criteria** (one `criteriaFor` per service, shared by the list and
> the export), so "respecting current filters" and "the same scope as the screen" hold by
> construction — `clinical-exports.e2e-spec.ts` asserts the patient export carries exactly the
> rows the list returns for the same filter. The administrator's patient export is the masked
> directory, asserted by searching the file for the clear name and MRN.
>
> - **One serialiser**, `common/http/csv-export.ts`: RFC 4180 quoting on every field, and
>   **formula-leading text** (`= + - @`, tab, CR) prefixed with `'` so a spreadsheet does not
>   evaluate a chart field (OWASP CSV injection). `text/csv` attachment, `Cache-Control: no-store`.
> - **Synchronous and bounded at 5,000 rows**; above it **409 `export_too_large`** and nothing is
>   written or audited. The contract's "async job → file → notification if large" is RA-M21.2's
>   carry: a generated file must pass M25.3's scan gate first.
> - **The single-record export** is a `Field,Value` CSV — the frontend's `recordSummaryDataset`
>   rows plus the sections it leaves out — care-scoped like the detail, with **signed notes only**.
> - **Audited once per file** as `patient.export` / `medical_record.export` / `prescription.export`
>   / `lab_order.export`, at `warning`, with the row count and the filter **names** (never a
>   search term).
> - Verified: `csv-export.spec.ts` (18) and `test/clinical-exports.e2e-spec.ts` (23). PDF export
>   (the frontend's print path) stays client-side.

## Milestone 11: Appointments

**Goal:** The scheduling engine (API §5).

> ⚠ *Multi-Hospital (2026-10-07):* patient booking is from doctor-published slots only (capacity 1, DB-level no double booking); working hours stay internal (DR-10, DR-11, OD-08). Phase 24, `BACKEND_CHECKLIST.md` §H.

- [x] **M11.1** — List/calendar queries (view/date/status/type/priority/q) + detail with `events[]` history.
  - ✅ Verify: list DTO carries the denormalized patient snapshot verbatim.
  - **Verified:** `GET /appointments` serves the six-field snapshot API §5.1 fixes — `{id, name, mrn, phone, age, gender}` — from a join rather than from copied columns, so a patient who changes their phone number does not leave an old one on an appointment booked before the move. `view=list` paginates; `day`, `week` and `month` are anchored on `date` and return the whole window, because a page-2 link on a week view means nothing. The scope is `doctorId` in the WHERE clause, asserted by a spec in which two doctors each hold one appointment and each list returns exactly their own. `GET /appointments/{id}` adds `events[]` newest-first with the `actor_name` snapshot (ADR-003 §4) and any outstanding proposals. `availableActions` is on every payload — API §5 forbids the client computing it — and a cross-check in `appointment-status.machine.spec.ts` proves no offered action is one the matrix would refuse.
- [x] **M11.2** — Create with conflict-check vs availability + overlapping bookings → 409.
  - ✅ Verify: overlap test → 409 `conflict`.
  - **Verified:** ADR-005 §4's five rules implemented as pure logic in `slot-conflict.ts` and wired to the grid by `SlotConflictChecker`. 23 unit cases cover the boundaries the ADR calls "the whole risk": a slot ending exactly at `end_time` is valid and one a minute later is not; a slot touching a break's edge is not inside it; the buffer widens the existing booking at **both** edges; a slot crossing midnight is refused rather than checked against its start day. The e2e half proves the wiring: outside hours → 409, inside the lunch break → 409, closed day → 409, overlap → 409 naming the clashing `APT-…` reference **and no patient** (asserted against the serialized body). A zero or negative `durationMin` is 422 with a field path rather than 409 — the body is wrong, not the book. Civil time is resolved in the clinic zone throughout: a 08:00 UTC slot is inside a 09:00–17:00 Cairo grid and outside a UTC one, and the weekday itself differs by zone (the Kiritimati case).
- [x] **M11.3** — Action endpoints approve/reject/cancel/reschedule/complete/no-show implementing the transition matrix (reason-required destructive, compare-and-set, `appointment_events` row per transition, notifications **after commit** via outbox).
  - ✅ Verify: full matrix test (legal + illegal per status × slot-date); race test — two concurrent approves → one 200 one 409.
  - **Verified:** the matrix is swept 8 × 8 = **64 assertions** against a table transcribed independently of the implementation, plus the slot-date pass. The clock gate is a **separate** refusal (`slot_not_reached`) from an illegal transition (`invalid_status_transition`), because "not yet" and "never" are different things for a client to say — and both are asserted through HTTP. The 409 `detail` speaks the kebab-case wire vocabulary, never the Prisma member. The machine is deliberately **stricter** than the M5.10 trigger, which also permits `pending → scheduled`: a backstop may be looser than the rule it backs; the reverse would be the defect. The race test runs two concurrent approves and gets `[200, 409]`, and a second case proves the loser wrote **no** event row — an event for a transition that did not happen is a lie in the history. Every `outbox.publish` is sequenced after the transaction it describes has returned.
- [x] **M11.4** — Proposals: propose-slots (≤3) → `awaiting-patient`; re-confirmation semantics.
  - ✅ Verify: proposal lifecycle test.
  - **Verified:** `POST …/propose-slots` accepts 1–3 slots, checks **every** one before offering any (a proposal the patient accepts and the server then refuses is the worst outcome this endpoint has — asserted: a refused slot leaves the appointment `pending` with zero proposals), writes the DB §S3 row, and moves the appointment to `awaiting-patient`. Resolving the appointment in either direction expires the outstanding proposal, so a patient is never looking at a choice that no longer exists. Origin states are **`pending` only**, per ADR-001 RA-001.1's instruction to take the conservative reading until product confirms the wider set; a proposal from `scheduled` is 409 and the spec asserts it.
- [x] **M11.5** — Emergency time-off: impact list → per-appointment/bulk resolution → blast-radius confirmation (`confirmedCount` must match) → outboxed notifications.
  - ✅ Verify: mismatch in confirmedCount → 409; nothing sent before confirmation (outbox empty).
  - **Verified:** `POST /me/time-off/{blockId}/resolve`, in the **appointments** module rather than the doctors one — the path is `/me/…` but what it writes is the appointment book, and M2.2 zone 1 is about tables, not URLs. A `confirmedCount` mismatch is 409 `blast_radius_changed`, and the spec then re-reads every impacted appointment to prove **nothing moved** — which is the verify step stated exactly. Each appointment resolves in its own transaction and the response separates `resolved` from `skipped`, so one lost compare-and-set does not roll back the other twenty-nine nor get silently counted as done. ⚠️ **One resolution reaches fewer rows than its name suggests, and it is recorded rather than papered over:** `reschedule-proposal` needs `awaiting-patient`, which ADR-001 permits only from `pending` (RA-001.1) and which the M5.10 trigger independently restricts the same way — so a `scheduled` appointment inside an emergency block is returned in `skipped` naming the refused transition, not forced through. A spec asserts both halves (skipped from `scheduled`, applied from `pending`) so that resolving RA-001.1 later is a deliberate decision rather than a gap quietly closing. See the residual note below.
- [x] **M11.6** — Admin oversight: cross-doctor list with `ageInState` + flags, nudge (templated, logged), reassign (same specialty, live availability, both parties notified), administrative cancel.
  - ✅ Verify: reassign to a different-specialty doctor → 422; all actions audited. **Live consumer exists** — ADM-05 shipped (`features/admin/appointments/`, 52 tests), including a 60-minute nudge cooldown and per-party delivery receipts the server must now own.
  - **Verified:** `GET /admin/appointments` under `appointments.reassign`, as its own service rather than a nullable scope on the doctor's — an unscoped read is a different authorization decision, and a parameter that widens a scope when it happens to be null is one forgotten argument from serving every doctor's book. A doctor holding only `appointments.manageOwn` gets 403. **The patient stays masked:** the row carries the `PT-…` handle and no identity fields at all (API §0.4), asserted by looking for the actual name in the serialized body rather than trusting the shape. `ageInStateHours` is derived from the event trail at read time — a stored age is wrong one minute after it is written (DB §14.5-6). Reassign enforces all three rules: a different specialty is **422 with a `doctorId` field error** as this verify step requires, an unavailable slot on the receiving doctor's own grid is 409 `slot_conflict` (the same five rules a fresh booking goes through), and the same doctor is 409. **The 60-minute nudge cooldown is now the server's**, from the last `nudged` event: ADM-05's disabled button is a courtesy, and a rule that lives only in a button lets a second administrator on a second browser send the third reminder to the patient anyway. Administrative cancellation writes `{ administrative: true, category }` onto the event, so the trail says who overrode whom. All four writes audit.

**🔒 Exit gate M11:** every `availableActions()` path in the frontend works end-to-end against the API.
  - **Gate status 2026-09-13: API half complete and green; the UI half cannot run yet.** Every `availableActions()` branch is exercised against the API — the server computes the list and a cross-check proves each offered action is one the matrix accepts. The manual "works in the frontend" half is blocked on the same thing M10's gate is: `features/doctor/appointments` is still on its mock seam (M40.1), so there is no UI issuing these calls. **88 tests green** across four specs — `slot-conflict.spec.ts` (23), `appointment-status.machine.spec.ts` (80 assertions over the 8 × 8 sweep and the clock gate), `appointments-book.e2e-spec.ts` (20), `appointments-transitions.e2e-spec.ts` (26), `appointments-oversight.e2e-spec.ts` (22).
  - **Residual — RA-001.1 is now load-bearing, not theoretical.** ADR-001 left the origin states for `awaiting-patient` undecided and named the conservative reading. M11.4 and M11.5 have both now been built against it, and M11.5's `reschedule-proposal` is materially narrowed by it: the bulk emergency resolution cannot reach a `scheduled` or `confirmed` appointment, which is the common case inside a time-off block. Widening is small and mechanical — one line in `appointment-status.machine.ts`'s table, a migration relaxing `enforce_appointment_transition`, and the two specs that pin the current behaviour — but it is a **product decision about whether a confirmed booking may be reopened for the patient to re-pick**, and ADR-001 is explicit that it must not be invented. Raise it with the owner alongside ADR-007's PD-1…PD-5.
  - **Not built here, and deliberately:** `notification_deliveries` receipts per party (M19.5 owns the table and the channels; the outbox messages that feed it carry a `recipient` discriminator already), and the `complaint` oversight flag, whose source of truth is M20.7's safety-flag queue — `matchesFlag` returns no rows for it rather than inventing a heuristic that would put appointments in front of an administrator as complaints when nobody complained.

## Milestone 12: Medical Records

**Goal:** EHR documents (API §6). **Never hard-deleted; fully versioned.**

- [x] **M12.1** — List + detail (full clinical document with children).
  - ✅ Verify: detail matches `MedicalRecord` shape (contract test).
  - **Verified:** field names are `features/doctor/medical-records/model/types.ts` verbatim — a payload the frontend renames before rendering is a payload two teams will disagree about. The list carries the seven-field `RecordPatientSnapshot` plus `diagnoses[]` **primary first**, so the table header reads the right one rather than whichever row the database returned. The detail adds treatment plan, medications, allergies, notes, attachments, timeline and the latest vitals. Filters: `q`, status, priority, visit type, department, `diagnosisCode`, `patientId`, date range. `q` reaches the reference, the chief complaint and the patient's three identifiers, deliberately **not** the HPI or examination — a substring search over free clinical prose returns everything and helps nobody. **One deviation from the frontend union, stated rather than papered over:** `vitals` is `null`-able here. `MedicalRecord.vitals` is non-optional because the mock always produces one; the server cannot promise that, because vitals live in `patient_vital_signs` and a draft written before the nurse has taken observations genuinely has none. `0/0 mmHg` with a BMI of 0 is a clinical falsehood, not a missing value. Widening the union is recorded for M39.
- [x] **M12.2** — Create/update with children in transactions; timeline event per change; one-primary-diagnosis enforced.
  - ✅ Verify: transactional rollback test (child failure rolls back parent).
  - **Verified:** the rollback test is the one the verify step asks for and not the easy substitute. Two diagnoses sharing an ICD code violate `@@unique([record_id, icd_code_id])`, which fires **inside** the transaction after the parent row is inserted — the spec then re-lists and finds zero records, proving the parent went back with the child. (The unknown-ICD-code case is tested too, but it refuses *before* any write, so it proves the guard rather than the rollback.) One-primary is enforced **twice, deliberately**: the migration adds `record_diagnoses_one_primary`, a partial unique on `(record_id) WHERE is_primary` that M5.7 never added — `@@unique([record_id, icd_code_id])` stops the same code twice and says nothing about how many are primary, and two primaries would make `primaryDiagnosis()`'s `find(...)` return whichever row came back first, possibly differing between two loads of the same page. The index is the invariant; a Zod refinement turns the violation into the 422 with a field path, which is an error a client can render. The index allows **zero** primaries on purpose: a draft has diagnoses before one is chosen as the headline.
- [x] **M12.3** — Status lifecycle draft→active→completed→archived (guarded).
  - ✅ Verify: illegal jump → 409.
  - **Verified:** a shared-kernel machine, swept 4 × 4 against an independently transcribed table. The contract's arrow chain is read literally: no skipping (`draft → completed` would leave the record never having been active, which is the state the action queue and the summary cards count), no backward edge (a document that can be un-finalised is one whose "completed" means nothing — corrections are amendments with their own version row), `archived` terminal. The 409 names the wire vocabulary and the allowed next step. Editing a finalised record is a **separate** refusal, `record_not_editable`, so a client can say "this chart is finalised" rather than "that status change is illegal". `POST …/status` rather than `PATCH { status }`, and `PATCH` does not accept `status` at all: a client that could set it through the general update would bypass the machine and the timeline event that makes a transition reviewable would never be written.
- [x] **M12.4** — Notes: create, sign (content frozen after signing), `aiAssisted` provenance persists; unsigned notes feed the action queue.
  - ✅ Verify: editing a signed note → 409/403; unsigned note appears in M15.4 queue.
  - **Verified (first half; second half pending M15.4).** Signing is a compare-and-set on `signed: false`, so a second attempt is told the note is already signed (409 `note_already_signed`) rather than quietly moving `signedAt` forward and making the freeze look later than it was. **Only the author may sign** — 403 `not_note_author`, asserted with a colleague who genuinely *can* read the record (the patient is shared first, so the refusal under test is authorship, not care scope): a signature a colleague can apply is a signature that means nothing, and the contract has no countersignature concept to hang it on. "Editing a signed note → 409/403" is satisfied in its strongest form: **there is no note-edit endpoint at all**, so the content is immutable from the moment it is written and an amendment is a new note. `aiAssisted` survives signing (M16.6 — the only path from an assistant's output into the chart is a note a doctor signed, and this flag is what makes that reviewable). ⚠️ `unsignedNotes` is counted and served on `GET /medical-records/summary`, but the **M15.4 action queue does not exist yet**, so the second half of this verify step cannot run.
- [x] **M12.5** — Attachments (5 kinds) via files service + download.
  - ✅ Verify: attachment round-trip.
  - **Verified (2026-10-23) — the round trip, over HTTP against Postgres.** `test/record-attachments.e2e-spec.ts`, 16 cases: upload through `POST /files` (kind `record_attachment`) → `POST /medical-records/{id}/attachments { fileId, kind, name? }` → the attachment on the document with an `attachment-added` timeline event → `GET …/attachments/{attachmentId}/download` held at **403 `scan_pending`** while pending, the same code with its own message when infected, and a signed 60-second URL naming the object's **storage key** once clean, audited as `file.download`. The object store is the in-memory double `files.e2e-spec.ts` uses (RA-M25.1).
  - **One upload path, not two.** The bytes go through M25.1's `POST /files`, which owns per-kind validation, the server-computed checksum and the scan queue; this route links the upload, the shape messaging uses for `attachmentIds` (§9.4). The lab path (§8.11) predates M25.1 and takes base64 inline; it is unchanged.
  - **Linkable means the caller's own, live `record_attachment` upload, attached once.** Another account's upload, another kind's, a deleted one and an id never issued are **one 422 on `fileId`**, so the answer never says which ids exist. A second link of the same upload is **409 `attachment_already_linked`**; the file row is locked `FOR UPDATE` inside the transaction so two concurrent links cannot both see it unlinked.
  - **Editability is the note rule.** Checked before and again under the record's row lock: a completed record answers **409 `record_not_editable`** and gains nothing. ⚠️ This is a choice the contract does not make — a referral or consent form can arrive after an encounter is finalised, and today that goes on an amendment. If the owner wants post-finalisation attachments, it is one guard to lift; the lab attach-to-record path (§8.9) already does not check status.
  - **Download is `records.view`, care-scoped first.** A doctor outside the care relationship gets a 404 byte-identical to a missing record's — never `403 scan_pending`, which would confirm the attachment exists. The contract row's `records.edit` covers the upload; `GET /files/{id}/download` already lets a reader in scope fetch the same bytes.
  - **The scan gate is now one function** (`shared/files/scan-gate.ts`, `assertScanClean`) used by `/files`, message attachments and record attachments, each with its wording unchanged. Two hand-written copies of it are how the message download came to open for infected files before M25.3.
  - **The ledger gets the attachment id and kind, never the filename** — a filename is the field most likely to carry a patient's name. The outbox topic `medical_record.attachment_added` is listed with the record's other topics as published-without-a-rule (`UNSOURCED_TOPICS`).
- [x] **M12.6** — Versioning: every update stores a before/after diff; `GET …/versions`.
  - ✅ Verify: two edits → two diffs retrievable; DELETE endpoint does not exist (404 route test) and DB role has no DELETE grant.
  - **Verified (two of three).** New table `record_versions`, insert-only — no `updatedAt`, no `deletedAt`, because a version row that can be edited is not a history and one that can be deleted makes "nothing changed" indistinguishable from "the change was removed". A **diff, not a snapshot**: both consumers (UX 1.5.3's history and API §14's logs diff viewer) render what changed, and a snapshot per save would copy the whole document on every keystroke-sized edit. `version` is dense from 1, assigned inside the update's transaction; `@@unique([record_id, version])` turns two concurrent updates into one success and one `stale_write` rather than two rows sharing number 4. Only **genuine** differences are recorded — a `PATCH` re-sending a field unchanged is a client echoing its form state, so it is refused with `no_effective_change` rather than filling the history with rows a reviewer cannot act on. Child collections are named (`children: ['treatmentPlan']`) rather than diffed line by line: a per-line diff of ordered free text is a merge algorithm, a different feature nobody asked for. Three edits → versions `[3, 2, 1]` with correct before/after, asserted. The **404 route test is in** (`DELETE /medical-records/{id}` → 404, and the record is still readable afterwards) — asserted as a route test rather than trusted from reading the controller, because a `@Delete` added later would pass review far more easily than it would pass this. ⚠️ The **DB-role DELETE grant** is not done: role grants are M37.3's territory (alongside RLS on PHI tables) and there is no per-role grant scheme in the schema yet — every migration runs as the owner. Carried to M37.3.
- [x] **M12.7** — `POST …/report-issue` → admin queue.
  - ✅ Verify: report creates a queue row + admin notification.
  - **Verified (queue row; notification pending M19).** Writes a `record_issue_reports` row in `open`, indexed on `(status, created_at)` for the queue that will read it. Guarded by `records.view` **only**, not `records.edit`, and deliberately: reporting that a chart is wrong is what a clinician who may read it should be able to do, and requiring edit rights would mean the people most likely to spot an error in someone else's record are the ones who cannot report it. ~~⚠️ Two halves outstanding, neither in this milestone's gift: the **admin queue UI/endpoint** is M23, and the **admin notification** needs M19's outbox consumer and channels — the outbox message (`medical_record.issue_reported`) is published, and today's binding logs and discards it.~~
  - **Both halves built and verified (2026-11-05) — the verify step is now met in full.** **Notification:** `medical_record.issue_reported` has a rule (`notification-rules.ts`), sourced from API §6.9 and this verify step, routing to `administrators`; the event carries `issueId` and `recordId` and the body is `Record MR-…`, never the description. It left `UNSOURCED_TOPICS`. **Queue:** `GET /admin/record-issues` (status filter, oldest first, the patient as a `PT-…` handle only) and `PATCH /admin/record-issues/{id}` (`open → investigating → resolved`, `open → resolved`; shared status kernel; compare-and-set, so two concurrent takes are one 200 and one 409), `@Roles('admin')` as every M23 route, audited `record_issue.investigate` / `record_issue.resolve` (`admin_activity`). The UI half is not built (no frontend consumer). `test/record-issue-queue.e2e-spec.ts` (12, over HTTP against PostgreSQL 16).

**🔒 Exit gate M12:** record detail page renders from API; version history + audit trail complete. ⚠️ **Write paths have no UI** — `features/doctor/medical-records` contains zero `useMutation` (M39.5). Create/update/sign are verified by contract test until that lands.
  - **Gate status 2026-09-13: NOT PASSED — one sub-item unbuilt, three verify steps partially met.** Six of seven sub-items are complete and green: **55 tests** — `record-status.machine.spec.ts` (24) and `medical-records.e2e-spec.ts` (31) — with typecheck and lint at zero errors. Outstanding, each with its owner named above: ~~M12.5 attachments~~ (built 2026-10-23), the unsigned-note action queue (M15.4), the DELETE grant on the database role (M37.3), ~~the admin issue queue (M23) and the admin notification (M19)~~ (both built 2026-11-05, see M12.7). The "renders from API" half is blocked on the same thing M10's and M11's gates are — the feature is still on its mock seam (M40.1).
  - **Care scoping is what this milestone is most exposed to, and it is asserted rather than assumed.** The list scopes by the care-relationship join; every single-record path re-checks through `PatientScopeService`. The spec proves the two **agree** — they are different queries over one rule, and a disagreement is a disclosure on one side or a lockout on the other. Out-of-scope and genuinely-missing return byte-identical 404s (ADR-022), asserted by comparing the two response **bodies**, not just their status codes.
