## Milestone 37: Security Hardening

**Goal:** Pre-production security pass (OWASP ASVS L2).

> ⚠ *Multi-Hospital (2026-10-07):* add the two-hospital isolation gates G-1…G-8 (`docs/multi-hospital/SECURITY_AND_DATA_ISOLATION.md`), including grant and Break-Glass lifecycle (G-3), employment termination (G-4), AI retention (G-5) and RLS (G-8). Phase 30.

- [ ] **M37.1** — Dependency audit clean; Helmet headers verified; JSON limits; CORS exact-origin + credentials.
  - ✅ Verify: header snapshot test; audit report clean.
  - **JSON limits — done (2026-10-16); the box stays unticked for the dependency audit and the headers.** `common/http/body-limits.ts` sets the limit explicitly (100 kB, the value body-parser applied implicitly, so no accepted request changes), registered after CORS in `main.ts` and by the e2e harness. An oversized body used to answer **500 `internal_error`** logged as an unhandled exception; it is now **413 `payload_too_large`**, and an undecodable charset 415 `unsupported_media_type`, both in the API §0.3 envelope (`AllExceptionsFilter` trusts only `http-errors` marked `expose`, below 500). `test/request-limits.e2e-spec.ts` (6) + 4 filter unit cases; booted `dist/main.js` returns the 413 with the CORS headers. Full backend gate: 128 files, 2,523 tests pass. AST-G2's assistant-message and tool-argument caps wait on those routes.
  - **Helmet headers and CORS — done and verified (2026-10-24); the box stays unticked for four residual advisories.**
    - **Headers.** `common/http/security-headers.ts` registers Helmet first in `main.ts` and in the e2e harness, so the suites assert what ships. It uses Helmet's defaults (one-year HSTS with subdomains, `nosniff`, `no-referrer`, COOP/CORP `same-origin`, no `X-Powered-By`) with two API-specific changes: `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`. `test/http-hardening.e2e-spec.ts` snapshots all eight headers on a public 200, a guard's 401, a body-parser 413 and a CORS preflight. A policy applied only to successful responses has holes.
    - **CORS.** `applyCors` (the same file) is the exact-origin, credentialed registration that was inline in `main.ts`. The spec asserts that an allowed origin is echoed with `credentials: true`, that an unlisted origin gets **no** `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and that the preflight answers 204 with the header policy.
    - **Dependency audit.** Production went from **14 advisories (1 critical, 9 high, 4 moderate) to 4 (1 high, 3 moderate)**:
      - **critical** `proxy-addr` IP spoofing via IPv4-mapped addresses (express 4's `req.ip`, which feeds the audit rows' `ip_address` and rate limiting): new override `^2.0.8`.
      - `body-parser`, `multer`, `qs` and `express`: overrides raised to their patched releases within Nest 10's majors.
      - `prisma`, `@prisma/client` and `@prisma/adapter-pg`: 7.9 → **7.10.1**, which also clears `deepmerge-ts` and `mysql2`.
      - `nodemailer`: 6 → **10.2.1**. Three high advisories, including CRLF SMTP command injection. The real-SMTP e2e suites are green on it.
    - **A defect the Prisma bump exposed, and fixed.** The 7.10 adapter reports a unique violation by **index name** (`constraint.index = 'users_email_key'`) instead of by columns. `isUniqueViolation(error, 'email')` stopped matching, and a concurrent duplicate signup answered **500 instead of 409**. That is the silent failure `prisma-errors.ts` warned about. The helper now reads all three shapes, and `prisma-errors.spec.ts` pins each one with the meta 7.10.1 actually produced.
    - **`zod` 3.25 → 4.6.7 (2026-10-25), clearing the last high advisory** (GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5, memory exhaustion validating oversized constrained collections; fixed only in Zod 4). Production audit: **4 (1 high, 3 moderate) → 3 moderate**. The migration was 38 files, and typecheck found most of it — but not the part that mattered:
      - **A data-loss regression no test caught.** Zod 4 applies a field's `.default()` even after `.partial()` has made it optional, so every PATCH schema built as `create.partial()` filled in the create defaults. A staff rename reset `status` to `off-duty` and `permissions` to `[]`; a department or room rename reset its status (and the department head to `null`); an announcement edit reset its priority, unpinned it and cleared its expiry; correcting a primary emergency contact's phone demoted them. The full suite stayed **green** on the upgrade — nothing asserted what a PATCH body parses *to*. Found with a walker over all 203 exported schemas looking for `optional(default)`; nine fields in five schemas. Fixed with `common/validation/patch-schema.ts` (`patchOf`: unwrap each default, then make it optional), used by every PATCH form of a create schema, including `updateAllergySchema`, which has no defaults today. Pinned by `patch-schema.spec.ts`, `administration.schema.spec.ts`, `patient-tabs.schema.spec.ts` (each fails with the fix reverted), and over HTTP by the staff-rename case in `administration-center.e2e-spec.ts` (fails with the fix reverted: the PATCH answers `status: off-duty`).
      - **`.default()` after `.transform()` is no longer transformed.** The six boolean env flags (`booleanish.default('false')`) now default to the output value (`.default(false)`); the walker found no other default wrapping a transform.
      - `GET /reports/insights` named the unrefined filters as `analyticsQuerySchema.innerType().innerType()`, which was Zod 3's `ZodEffects` nesting; Zod 4 keeps refinements on the object. The base is now `insightsQuerySchema`, the same shape the route always accepted.
      - `settings.service` keeps Zod 3's `flatten().fieldErrors` shape (top-level key, slice-wide refinements dropped) with an explicit loop rather than re-deciding it in an upgrade.
      - **API-visible, deliberately not shimmed:** Zod's **default** message texts changed (`Required` → `Invalid input: expected string, received undefined`; `String must contain at least 1 character(s)` → `Too small: expected string to have >=1 characters`). Keys, codes and every custom message are unchanged. API §0.3 specifies `fieldErrors` keys, not message text; a client that displays a server message verbatim shows the new wording.
      - `z.string().uuid()` is RFC 9562-strict in Zod 4. Every id this API accepts is `gen_random_uuid()` (v4) or the nil UUID, both accepted; every literal in `prisma/` and `test/` is RFC-valid.
      - **Not done, deliberately:** Zod 4 deprecates `.email()`/`.uuid()`/`.datetime()` on `z.string()`, `.superRefine`, `.merge` and `message:` in favour of top-level formats and `error:`. They still work and are not flagged by lint; rewriting ~250 call sites — including `auth/dto/`, which `patient-app/src/test/parity.test.ts` reads by regex — is a separate change. `frontend/` and `patient-app/` still pin Zod 3 and carry the same advisory in their own trees.
    - ⚠️ **Residual, each needing a decision rather than a bump:**
      - `@nestjs/core` / `@nestjs/platform-express` (moderate). Fixed only in Nest 11+, which is a stack change ADR-015 defers.
      - `ajv` (moderate). ReDoS only with the `$data` option on untrusted schemas. It reaches production only through the Prisma CLI's `@prisma/dev` → `@prisma/streams-local` (a local-development helper the API never calls), and the app's own validation is Zod.
- [ ] **M37.2** — Pen-test checklist executed: token reuse/fixation, authz matrix re-run, injection, **SSRF (M24.4 test-connection)**, file-upload abuse, rate-limit bypass.
  - ✅ Verify: findings log with all criticals/highs resolved.
  - **SSRF — DNS rebinding closed (2026-10-25); the box stays unticked for the rest of the checklist.** `ssrf-guard.ts` deferred this here by name. `IntegrationService` resolved the host and checked every address, then the probe called `fetch`, which asked the resolver **again** — so a host answering public-then-loopback (a zero-TTL rebinding record) passed the check and was dialled at `127.0.0.1` or `169.254.169.254`. `HttpIntegrationProbe` (replacing `FetchIntegrationProbe`) sends the `HEAD` through `node:http`/`node:https` with a **guarded `lookup`**: the socket's own resolution checks every answer with `isBlockedAddress`, so the address checked is the address connected to and there is no second resolution to race. A mixed public+private answer is refused too (happy-eyeballs may dial either). `agent: false`, so no pooled socket skips the lookup; redirects still not followed; the service's pre-check stays outside the injectable token. `integration-probe.spec.ts` (11, real requests to a local server, only the resolver substituted): loopback at connect time → `BlockedAddressError` and **the server receives nothing**; the same for `https:` and the metadata address; a permitted probe sends exactly one `HEAD` with the original `Host`; a 302 is reported and not followed; a silent server times out. Disabling the connect-time check fails 4 of them. **Not exercised here:** a probe to a real public host — this environment's egress goes through a proxy that `node:http` (like `fetch` before it) does not use.
  - **Findings log (2026-10-25, source review plus the existing suites; no external scanner was run).** No critical or high finding is open after #114.
    | Area | Finding | Severity | State |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | SSRF (`test-connection`) | DNS rebinding between the guard's resolution and the socket's | High | **Fixed** (above) |
    | Token reuse / fixation | Refresh rotates on every use; a rotated token coming back revokes its session and leaves the user's others alone; a merely expired token does not trip reuse; the cookie is `httpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict; Path=/api/v1/auth`; access and refresh tokens are minted fresh at login, so there is no pre-login token to fix | — | Verified by `auth-refresh.e2e-spec.ts` and `auth-mobile-client.e2e-spec.ts` |
    | CSRF | The only cookie-authenticated route is `POST /auth/refresh`; `SameSite=Strict` keeps the cookie off cross-site requests and exact-origin CORS keeps the response unreadable | — | No finding |
    | Injection | Prisma's parameterised queries throughout; 15 `$queryRaw`/`$executeRaw` sites, all tagged templates; one `$executeRawUnsafe` (`DROP TABLE` of an audit partition), whose name comes from `pg_class` and is regex-checked | — | No finding |
    | NUL in text | PostgreSQL cannot store U+0000 in `text`, `varchar` or `jsonb` (values or keys), and Zod's `string()` accepts it. A NUL in **any** stored or queried string reached the database and answered **500**: the public `POST /auth/register` (`firstName`), a patient's name, an allergy, a record's chief complaint, the patient search's `q`, and a path parameter. Anyone could produce 500s and error-level log lines, without an account | Medium | **Fixed 2026-10-31** — `common/validation/reject-nul.pipe.ts`, one global pipe (`APP_PIPE`) that runs before every route's own validation on body, query and path values. It answers **422 `validation_failed`**, naming each field in the Zod pipe's path format (`allergies[0].allergen`). Refused rather than stripped: changing clinical text silently is worse than refusing it. The walk is iterative, so 100,000 levels of nesting do not overflow it. `reject-nul.pipe.spec.ts` (10) and `test/nul-input.e2e-spec.ts` (6); all five hostile cases fail with the pipe unregistered (500), and newlines, tabs and Arabic text still save. Upload names were probed too: busboy refuses a NUL in a part header with 400 before any route runs |
    | Authz matrix | Re-run as M34.2's route × role suite (286 routes) | — | Verified (M34.2) |
| Required 2FA | With `security.twoFactorRequired` on, sign-in issued a step-up challenge to an account that had **never enrolled**. There is no secret to verify against (`StepUpService.verify`: `secretFor` is null), so the user sat on a code screen where every code answered `otp_invalid` until the attempts ran out. Turning the policy on locked out every unenrolled user with a misleading error. ADR-035 §7 already refuses this state for administrators for exactly that reason ("a step-up would mint a code nobody can produce"), and the policy path skipped the rule. The test asserted only that the challenge was offered | Medium | **Fixed 2026-10-30** — `login.service.ts`: under the policy an unenrolled account gets the administrator backstop's **403 `two_factor_required`** (a code the frontend already handles), after the password check; enrolled accounts are stepped up as before. `auth-login.e2e-spec.ts`: enrolled → step-up; unenrolled → 403, with no challenge row and no session (fails against the old code); wrong password still 401 first. **Still open, a product decision:** how an unenrolled user enrols while the policy is on. `/me/2fa/setup` needs a session, so today an administrator turns the policy off, or the user enrols before it is turned on |
    | Cache policy | **No API response sets `Cache-Control`**, PHI included (`GET /patients`, records, labs). Helmet does not set it. A shared cache will not store a response to a request carrying `Authorization` (RFC 9111 §3.5), but a browser may keep one in its private cache, and ASVS 8.2.1 asks that sensitive data not be cached by browsers | Medium | **Resolved 2026-11-05 (M32.3): (c) with `no-store` as the default**, which needed no route classification beyond four PHI-free reference routes. Every response is `no-store` (errors, 401s and auth included); `/meta/enums`, the formulary list and monograph and the lab catalogue are `private, no-cache` (revalidated, never in a shared cache), and only on success. Badge counts stay `no-store`: the body (~50 B) is smaller than the 304. No client relies on browser caching. `common/http/cache-policy.ts`, `test/cache-policy.e2e-spec.ts`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` §13. *Original analysis:* the options trade against M32.3. (a) `Cache-Control: no-store` on every authenticated response: ASVS-conformant, but browsers then never store a response, so the ETag above saves nothing for the web client's badge polling. (b) `private, no-cache`: always revalidated, ETags work, but PHI may sit in the browser's disk cache. (c) `no-store` on PHI routes and `private, no-cache` on non-PHI polling routes (badge counts, `/meta/enums`): needs a route classification. **Recommendation: (c)**, with `no-store` the default so a new route is safe unless marked. Not implemented |
| Idle timeout | The administrator's **Session timeout (minutes)** (`security.sessionTimeoutMinutes`, default 30) is validated, stored and resolved by `PolicyService`, and **nothing reads it**. The server enforces no idle timeout: sessions end at the refresh TTL, 12 h or 30 d, sliding. It is not in the login or refresh payload either (`AuthenticatedPayload` is `user`, `accessToken`, `expiresIn`), so the web client's idle lock stays at its 10-minute built-in default (`frontend/src/configs/session.ts`). The ADR on session lifetimes ("Idle lock — `security.sessionTimeoutMinutes`") and that file's comment both say the client takes the value "from the session payload". Changing the setting has no effect anywhere. `SessionService.touch()`, which would maintain `sessions.last_active_at` for a server-side check, has no caller | Medium (an administrator-visible security control that does nothing) | **Open — needs a contract decision.** Either (a) serve it in the login/refresh payload under a field name the web and mobile clients agree to read, which is the ADR's reading, or (b) also enforce it server-side at refresh against `last_active_at`. (b) is not a substitute for (a): the web client refreshes silently every 15 minutes while open, so an idle-but-open tab never looks idle to the server, and a timeout under `JWT_ACCESS_TTL` would end every session at its first refresh. The field name and shape are the frontend's and the mobile client's to agree, so nothing was added |
    | Malformed refresh cookie | `readRefreshCookie` passed the client's `medora_rt` value to `decodeURIComponent`, which throws `URIError` on a malformed escape. An unauthenticated `POST /auth/refresh` with `medora_rt=%`, `%zz` or a truncated UTF-8 escape answered **500** and logged an unhandled exception, which anyone could produce at will (log and alert noise). Malformed escapes in path parameters already answer 400, and query strings are tolerated | Low | **Fixed 2026-10-31** — a value that does not decode is returned as it is. Issued tokens are base64url and never contain `%`, so it matches no session and the refresh is the ordinary 401; a present cookie still wins over a mobile body token. `refresh-cookie.spec.ts` (4) and two `auth-mobile-client.e2e-spec.ts` cases, all three new behaviours failing against the old parser |

Removing the pin fails the HS512 case |
    | Dates out of range | `z.coerce.date()` accepts any year JavaScript holds (±275,760); the database refuses far less. Thirteen request schemas use `z.coerce.date()` | Low | **Fixed 2026-10-31** — `ZodValidationPipe` walks the *parsed* output and refuses a `Date` outside years 0001–9999 with **422** naming the field. The bound is the API's own: every instant it serves is four-digit-year ISO-8601 (M41.2), so it could not hand such a date back. Plausibility inside the range stays each field's rule. `zod-validation.pipe.spec.ts` (4) and `test/date-range.e2e-spec.ts` (4); the three refusals fail against the old pipe, and both ends of the range save and are served back |

Tested: parser, guard, counter against real Redis, and over HTTP — including that a forged `X-Forwarded-For` is ignored by default and believed only as far as a trusted hop. Not tested: the production proxy's real header behaviour |
    | Integer overflow | `POST`/`PATCH /administration/resources` took `quantity` and `reorderLevel` as `z.number().int().min(0)`, which allows up to 2^53; both columns are PostgreSQL `integer`, and 2,147,483,648 answered **500**. A scan of every request schema found no other stored integer without a bound under `integer`'s limit (the only `BigInt` columns are file sizes the server computes) | Low (administrator routes) | **Fixed 2026-10-31** — both capped at the column's limit, 2,147,483,647, which is accepted; one more is 422 naming the field (`administration-center.e2e-spec.ts`, failing against the old schema) |

- [ ] **M37.3** — PHI-leak scan of logs; RLS enabled on PHI tables (M4.4 ADR follow-through).
  - ✅ Verify: RLS policy tests (doctor A vs B) pass at the DB level.
  - **Three grants are owed to this sub-item by earlier milestones**, each deferred here deliberately rather than improvised where it was noticed. There is no per-role grant scheme in the schema yet — every migration runs as the owner — so all three need the role model this milestone builds.
    1. **M12.4's DELETE grant** on the medical-record tables. The route-level refusal is done and tested (`DELETE /medical-records/{id}` → 404); the database-level one is not.
    2. **M16.6's clinical-write denial** for the AI module. `ai-containment.spec.ts` asserts it against the *source* — no write method on any clinical model, `AiContextRepository` carrying no write method at all, no raw SQL — and is proven load-bearing by injection. That is a check which fails in review, on the commit that introduces the write, and names the file; it is **not** a check that survives a compromised process. The grant is the half only this milestone can add, and the two are complements rather than substitutes.
    3. **The PHI-log scan** should include the AI transcript log (`shared/ai/ai-transcript.log.ts`), which by construction records shape and cost and never prompt or completion text. The scan is what keeps that true rather than merely intended.
  - **PHI-log scan, error objects — fixed (2026-10-30); the box stays unticked for RLS and the three grants above.** A source review of every logger call site, then a probe against the real adapter, found two leaks and one censored diagnostic:
    - **A failed Prisma write logged the row.** Twelve call sites log `{ err: error }`, and pino's default serialiser copies every enumerable property. Both audit writers logged a Prisma error's full `message`, which for a `PrismaClientValidationError` prints the arguments with their values. `@prisma/adapter-pg` 7.10 puts Postgres's `DETAIL` at `err.meta.driverAdapterError.cause.detail` — observed for a CHECK violation in `LoginEventService`: `Failing row contains (…, khaled.nassar@example.com, …)`. No redaction path matches `detail`. Only the exception filter was safe, because it described the error itself before logging.
    - **A refused recipient reached the log.** `SmtpMailer.logFailure` says it records the domain and never the address, but it logged nodemailer's error, which quotes a refused recipient in `message`, `response` and `rejected[]`.
    - **Every 5xx line read `[redacted]` where its cause should be.** `redaction.ts` censors `name` (an identifier) and `code` (an OTP) one level down in every logged object, `err` included, so the filter's `err.name` and `err.code` never reached the log store.
    - **Fix.** The filter's description moved to `common/logging/error-description.ts` and became the logger's `err` serialiser: `type`, `errorCode`, `stack`, and `message` except for Prisma errors. Nothing else is copied, so a library adding a property cannot add a leak. The two keys are renamed because a pino wildcard cannot exempt one container. The two audit writers use it in place of their own copies. The mailer now logs only the error's class, code, `responseCode` and `command`, as `smtp.type`/`smtp.errorCode` (**corrected 2026-10-31** after post-merge verification: the first version used `name`/`code`, which the same wildcards censored to `[redacted]`. Its spec read the logger call's argument rather than pino's output; it now reads both).
    - **Verified.** `error-description.spec.ts` (7) runs through pino-http built from the real `buildLoggerOptions`; 4 of its cases fail with the serialiser unregistered. `smtp.mailer.spec.ts` (3) uses an in-test relay that refuses `RCPT TO` with a real 550, so the error is nodemailer's own. **Post-merge check (2026-10-31):** the built `dist/main.js`, run with real pino output against PostgreSQL, logged a driver error from the health indicator as `type`/`errorCode` with the Prisma message withheld. A refused mail logged no address, and the whole log contained no `[redacted]` once the mailer keys were corrected. **Not covered:** messages written by hand into a log line's text (`permission-resolver.service.ts` logs `asMessage(error)` as the message). Those are a Prisma *invocation* message keyed by user id, not a row, and are left as they are.
  - **PHI-log scan extended to the AI transcript log (2026-11-14); the box stays unticked — RLS on PHI tables and the three database grants above are DB-blocked (they need migrations and an application role distinct from the owner) and are not touched here.** `shared/ai/ai-transcript.log.spec.ts` (6 cases) holds grant 3 above: the declared fields of `AiTranscriptRecord` are an exact allow-list (ids, a `patientBound` boolean, counts, short tokens — a `prompt`, `completion` or `patientId` field fails by name); every `transcript.record(...)` call site under `src/` must pass a plain literal of allow-listed keys (read with the TypeScript compiler API, so a spread of a variable or an extra key fails and names the file); and the line production pino writes, through the real `buildLoggerOptions`, carries only allow-listed fields whose values are of the allowed kind and is not censored to `[redacted]`. **Proven load-bearing** against scratch copies, since deleted: an added `prompt` field and a call site passing `patientName` both fail it. **No leak found today** — the logger records shape and cost only. **One latent weakness, closed:** `record()` spread its argument into the log line, so a caller that bypassed the type (a cast, a variable) would have written unlisted fields. It now copies each allow-listed field by name; the sixth case passes a cast entry carrying a `prompt`, a `completion` and a `patientId` and asserts none reaches the line (it fails against the spreading version). Valid callers' lines are unchanged.
- [ ] **M37.4** — Incident-response runbook drafted.
  - ✅ Verify: runbook reviewed + committed.
  - **Drafted and committed 2026-10-30; the box stays unticked until the owner reviews it.** [`INCIDENT_RESPONSE.md`](INCIDENT_RESPONSE.md) covers the first hour, a proposed severity scale, and containment for a compromised account, a leaked `JWT_SECRET` or `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, an exposed database, an attack on sign-in, maintenance mode, a leaked API key and suspected misuse of patient data. It also covers investigation from a 5xx `ref` to the logs and the ledger, evidence export, and close-out. Every control names its route, setting or command and the test that exercises it; each was checked against the code. **Not rehearsed** against any deployment. Its §7 lists the gaps it found: no route revokes every session (platform-wide sign-out is SQL plus a `JWT_SECRET` rotation); no bulk forced password reset; a sole administrator cannot be contained through the API; and no containment exists for a leaked `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY`. It also names the owner's decisions: severity, on-call, and breach-notification duties.

**🔒 Exit gate M37:** security sign-off recorded.

## Milestone 38: Production Readiness

**Goal:** Operability.

- [ ] **M38.1** — Monitoring dashboards (uptime, latency percentiles, error rate, queue depth — **feeding the M23.6 System-Performance widgets**) + alerting rules + log retention.
  - ✅ Verify: dashboards live; a forced error fires an alert; `/administration/platform-status` reads these metrics rather than constants.
  - **The application's half — built 2026-10-30; the box stays unticked: no dashboard, alerting rule or log-retention policy exists, and none can be built without a monitoring backend.** `shared/metrics` keeps in-process metrics, rendered in the Prometheus text format at **`GET /api/v1/metrics`**:
    - `medora_http_requests_total{method,route,status_class}` and a `medora_http_request_duration_seconds` histogram (11 buckets, 5 ms to 10 s), recorded by Express middleware registered **before** the body parsers. Unmatched routes and parser refusals (413/415) are counted too, which an interceptor would miss.
    - Process gauges (uptime, RSS, heap) and the job kernel's counters (`medora_job_runs_total`, `medora_job_last_duration_seconds`).
    - `route` is the **matched pattern** (`/api/v1/patients/:patientId`), never the path a client sent, so no identifier reaches a label and series count stays bounded. A wildcard middleware mount counts as `<unmatched>`.
    - **Off unless `METRICS_TOKEN` is set** (404 while unset; 401 for a wrong bearer, compared in constant time). The route is `@Public()` for the scraper and added to the reviewed public list in `authz-route-matrix.e2e-spec.ts`.
    - Per instance, by design (Prometheus aggregates). That is why `/administration/platform-status` (M23.6) still answers 501: one instance's latency shown as "the platform's" would mislead.
    - Verified: `metrics.registry.spec.ts` (5), `metrics.controller.spec.ts` (the 404), `test/metrics.e2e-spec.ts` (6 over HTTP). **Not verified:** any scraper or backend — none is configured.
- [ ] **M38.2** — Backup automation per platform settings + **restore drill executed**; DR runbook (RPO ≤ 24 h, RTO ≤ 4 h).
  - ✅ Verify: restore drill log committed.

Reads `PolicyService.current()` (database over `MAINTENANCE_MODE`), so it takes effect on every instance without a restart. `@Public()` routes (sign-in, refresh, registration, invitation, health) stay open so an administrator can obtain a session and orchestrator probes keep answering. `test/maintenance-mode.e2e-spec.ts`, 9 cases: doctor 503 `service_unavailable` with the configured message and a `ref`; default message when blank; writes refused; on/off immediate; admin 200; health not 503; login 200; unauthenticated still 401. Full backend gate: 128 files, 2,522 tests pass. Cost: one `platform_settings` read per authenticated non-admin request until M31 caches policy.

  - ✅ Verify: prod seed script contains no `Medora123!` / OTP `123456` (grep in CI). **The root README publishes these demo credentials — confirm they exist only as mock fixtures and never reach a real environment.**
  - **Verified 2026-11-14 — the grep runs in CI as a test, with no workflow change.** `test/production-seed-credentials.e2e-spec.ts` (5 cases) runs in the backend job's existing `npm run test` step. It walks the **import graph** of `prisma/seed-production.ts` rather than a file list, so a file added to that path later is checked automatically, and asserts: the demo layer (`prisma/seed/demo.ts`) is not in the graph; no file in it carries `Medora123!`, the mock OTP `123456` as a standalone literal, or a PHC-format argon2 hash; and the same holds for all of `src/` (the application and its CLIs, specs excluded). A fifth case proves the patterns are load-bearing (they match `demo.ts` and a literal OTP, and do not match a longer number or the hash format named in prose). That answers the README question on the backend side: `Medora123!` exists only in the demo layer, which the production path never loads, and in test fixtures. The decision recorded below as "still open" was taken on 2026-10-30 (option (b), `admin:bootstrap`, implemented above), and the catalogue-overwrite concern is answered by the production seed's `create-only` mode. **Not verified, and not part of this item's verify step:** a run against a production database, which needs the owner's authorization.
  - **Status 2026-10-25 — a prerequisite fixed; the item itself stays open on a decision.**
    - **Fixed: a re-seed re-granted every capability an administrator had revoked.** The matrix editor revokes by deleting the `role_permissions` row; `seedReferenceData` upserted every bundle grant, so the next `db:seed` silently re-created it, with no audit row. The seed's own comment said it must never do that. It now seeds a grant only where this run created the role or the permission — the baseline a new role or capability needs — and leaves every other pair as the database has it. `test/seed-reference-data.e2e-spec.ts` (3 cases, each in a rolled-back transaction): a revoked grant stays revoked (fails on the old seed); a capability the database has never seen is granted to exactly the bundles naming it; an already-matching database is untouched, `granted_at` included. Also run as the real script: fresh database → grants equal the bundles; re-run → unchanged; delete pharmacy's `messages.send`, re-seed → still absent. **Cost, accepted:** a bundle edited in code to add an *existing* capability reaches only databases seeded afterwards; on a live database that is an authorization change for the matrix, not for a seed.
    - **Implemented 2026-10-30, at the owner's instruction ("implement a secure, explicit bootstrap procedure"; option (a) above, as a separate command rather than inside the seed).** The two halves of the decision below are now built; the setup and rotation procedure is [`PRODUCTION_SETUP.md`](PRODUCTION_SETUP.md).
      - **`npm run db:seed:production`** (`prisma/seed/production.ts`) — permissions, roles, vocabularies, catalogues and platform settings; the demo layer is not imported; no user or credential is created; **create-only** (`prisma/seed/seed-mode.ts`), so a rerun after go-live keeps an administrator's role renames and catalogue corrections. That also answers the catalogue-overwrite concern below *for production*; `db:seed` keeps refreshing content in development and staging, and still refuses production.
      - **`npm run admin:bootstrap -- --email <address> --confirm`** (`src/cli/`, `AdminBootstrapService`) — issues one administrator invitation with no sender (`admin_invitations.invited_by` now nullable, migration `20261030090000_admin_bootstrap_invitation`), mailed by default or printed with `--print-link`. The invitee completes ADR-035's two phases, so the only path to an ACTIVE administrator is still the one that verified a TOTP code. Refuses before roles exist, while **any** administrator exists, and for a taken address; `--reissue` removes only an accepted-but-never-completed bootstrap account. No HTTP route, no default credential, no password written.
      - **Verified:** `test/admin-bootstrap.e2e-spec.ts` (7 cases, on a database created empty for the file and dropped after it) and the compiled command run by hand against a scratch database. **Not verified:** real SMTP delivery, and anything against production — running it there needs the owner's authorization.
      - ~~**Still open:** `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY` cannot be rotated (one key, no re-encryption path; rotating it locks out every two-factor account).~~ **Rotatable since 2026-11-06, with no lockout window** (`PRODUCTION_SETUP.md` §2.1). This is the engineering item the 2026-10-30 decision brief left out of its decisions. No owner decision, migration or schema change was needed: every envelope column is already `bytea`.
        - **Keyring.** `EnvelopeService` encrypts only under `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, and decrypts under it or any key in the new optional `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS` (comma-separated). Boot refuses a malformed entry, a repeated one, and the current key listed as previous.
        - **Keyed format, opt-in.** A keyed envelope is `0x02 ‖ keyId(8) ‖ iv ‖ tag ‖ ciphertext`, with the header bound as GCM additional data. `keyId` is a domain-separated SHA-256 fingerprint of the key. Both layouts are always read; header-less ones are tried under each key in the ring, current first, and GCM authentication picks the key. **Only legacy is written by default** (`DATA_ENCRYPTION_WRITE_FORMAT=legacy`, review finding F2), because a build from before the keyring cannot open a keyed envelope. `keyed` is an explicit phase 2 (`PRODUCTION_SETUP.md` §2.1).
        - **Re-encryption.** `EnvelopeReencryptionService` and `npm run crypto:reencrypt -- (--dry-run | --confirm)` re-seal all four envelope columns (`user_credentials.totp_secret_enc`, `.recovery_codes_enc`, `platform_integrations.config_enc`, `user_integrations.oauth_tokens_enc`). Each row is a compare-and-set on the bytes read, so a concurrent re-enrolment is kept rather than overwritten. A value no key opens is never written, and its id is reported. The command pages by primary key and is idempotent. Exit 0 means a complete verification pass found every value conforming **at that moment**. That is a point-in-time result, not permission to remove the previous key, which also requires the retirement preconditions in `PRODUCTION_SETUP.md` §2.1: every envelope writer upgraded and drained before the run started, in-flight re-enrolment tokens expired, and the old key kept offline. Audit rows (security, warning): `encryption_key.reencrypt_started`, then `encryption_key.reencrypted` or `encryption_key.reencrypt_failed`.
        - **Verified.** `envelope.service.spec.ts` (22), `env.spec.ts` (+6), `reencrypt-envelopes.args.spec.ts` (13), and `test/envelope-key-rotation.e2e-spec.ts` (7) on a database created for the file. The e2e boots the application as the CLI does, with the new key current and the old one previous, over legacy, keyed-old, keyed-new, current-key-legacy and lost-key envelopes, plus a re-enrolment injected between read and write. It shows: sign-in's `secretFor` still reads old-key secrets before re-encryption; a dry run writes and audits nothing; a confirmed run leaves everything readable under the new key alone, keeps the concurrent rewrite, and leaves the unreadable value byte-identical; a rerun writes nothing; the report turns exit 0 once nothing remains; and the column list equals the schema's `bytea` columns. Two checks fail when the protection is removed: dropping the compare-and-set fails the concurrent case, and dropping the previous keys fails 8 cases. The compiled `node dist/cli/reencrypt-envelopes.js` was run by hand against a local scratch database (usage exit 2, dry run exit 0).
        - **Independent review (2026-11-06) and remediation (2026-11-07).** The review found F1–F11 and reproduced five of them (P3, P4, P5, P6, P7). F1–F7 are fixed:
          - **F1.** Exit 0, and the "previous key can be emptied" message, now need a complete read-only verification pass after every write, plus no stale concurrent write. A lost compare-and-set is re-read and classified (removed, current, unreadable, or stale and retried), never assumed.
          - **F2.** Legacy writes by default; keyed is opt-in.
          - **F3, F4.** A rolling-safe rotation order (pre-stage, then swap) and a rollback table.
          - **F5.** `reencrypt_started` is written before the first write, and the run refuses to start without it. The outcome is `reencrypted` (complete or incomplete) or `reencrypt_failed` with partial counts. All go through the strict ledger writer, so a failed write is reported (exit 4), not claimed.
          - **F6.** A session advisory lock on a dedicated connection, checked before every page; a second run gets exit 3.
          - **F7.** Pages stop only when the driving query is empty. The `user_integrations` driving query is `groupBy … LIMIT`, which also fixes F8's unbounded read.
          - **Evidence.** 19 cases in `test/envelope-key-rotation-hardening.e2e-spec.ts`; 8 more in `envelope.service.spec.ts`, 3 in `env.spec.ts`; and the existing rotation e2e updated to the new report. Seven mutations, each reintroducing one defect, each fail a regression test.
          - **Still documented only:** F9 (`updated_at` changes on every re-sealed row), F10 (the coverage test checks only `bytea` columns), F11 (in-flight re-enrolment tokens, valid for `OTP_CHALLENGE_TTL_SECONDS`, now 10 minutes).
        - **Final review (2026-11-07, R1–R8) and remediation.** R1–R6 and R8 are fixed; R7 is documented.
          - **R1, R4.** Exit 0 is reported as point-in-time. Only `--confirm` states the retirement preconditions: every envelope writer upgraded and drained *before* the run started (API replicas, workers, jobs, autoscaling templates, old task definitions, other writers). A dry run never authorizes retirement.
          - **R2.** Writes run on the lock's own session, guarded in-statement by the lock and the backend pid. After a lock loss nothing more is written.
          - **R3.** Transaction-mode pooling is refused (exit 6) on pid drift, a re-entrant lock or backend affinity. Detection is best effort; the pid-bound write guard is what makes a missed detection safe. Verified against a local PgBouncer 1.22 in both modes.
          - **R5.** SIGTERM/SIGINT stop between rows and record `interrupted` (exit 5). `--status` lists started runs with no outcome.
          - **R6.** Failure records carry every counter, and `uncertainWrites` makes the counts lower bounds.
          - **R8.** Connect, query, statement and row-lock timeouts plus TCP keepalive on the lock connection, failing closed.
          - **Evidence.** `test/envelope-key-rotation-safety.e2e-spec.ts` (16) and `src/cli/reencrypt-signals.spec.ts` (3), plus updated assertions in both earlier rotation suites. Eight mutations, each failing a regression test.
          - **Not production-ready.** Exit 0 still depends on operational preconditions the code cannot check.
        - **Not covered.** No route clears another account's second factor, so an *unreadable* factor needs database access to reset, and an administrator in that state has no supported way back. A leak of the key **and** the database still needs every account to re-enrol (`INCIDENT_RESPONSE.md` §3.3). Nothing was run against a production database.
    - ~~**Still open, and an owner/security decision rather than engineering:**~~ *(Superseded 2026-10-30: the owner chose option (b) below, implemented as `admin:bootstrap`; kept for the record.)* The seed refuses `APP_ENV=production` outright, so a production database has no way to receive the reference data, roles, catalogues and platform settings the seed header says every environment needs — and ADR-017 §2 / ADR-035 say the first administrator "must be seeded". **How that administrator gets a first credential is undecided.** No password may be seeded (this item); ADR-035 rejects a bootstrap endpoint; the invitation flow (which enforces MFA by construction) cannot be reused as-is because `admin_invitations.invited_by` is a required FK to an existing user. Options for the owner: (a) make `invited_by` nullable and have the production seed issue one invitation, printing the link once to the operator; (b) a one-shot CLI that does the same outside the seed; (c) seed the user with no credential and let them set one through `forgot-password` (no MFA enrolment forced, unlike (a)/(b)). Separately, the catalogue seed deliberately overwrites catalogue *content* on every run ("a reference range corrected upstream should propagate"), which on a live database would also overwrite an administrator's catalogue edits — the owner should confirm that before the seed runs against production.

**🔒 Exit gate M38:** ops sign-off recorded.

**Goal:** Land the frontend prerequisites that integration depends on. Revision 1's M34 assumed every feature had a mock seam ready to swap. It does not: four feature modules have no write paths at all, five admin screens do not exist, and the transport layer drops the error `code` the auth pages branch on. **This is frontend work** — schedule it with the frontend owner; the backend milestones above are verified by contract test until it lands.

*Evidence: `frontend/FRONTEND_CHECKLIST.md` §0 (the three real problems), §4 (admin dashboard), DP-02/DP-04.*

Without this, every login failure becomes `{kind:'auth'}` and the pages cannot tell `invalid_credentials` from `email_unverified`.
  - ✅ Verify: `LoginPage`/`VerifyOtpPage` branch on `error.code` from a real HTTP response; the eight codes in `features/auth/mock/types.ts` all round-trip.

New table-driven case in `frontend/src/api/endpoints/auth.test.ts` drives every code the server raises, at the status it raises it with (401 `invalid_credentials`/`otp_invalid`/`token_invalid`, 403 `email_unverified`/`account_inactive`/`two_factor_required`, 409 `email_taken`/`professional_number_taken`, 422 `token_invalid`/`weak_password`, 429 `rate_limited`), from an HTTP body through the real adapter to the coded `AuthError` (11/11). `otp_required` is a 200 challenge, never an error body. Page branching: `LoginPage.test.tsx`, `VerifyOtpPage` tests. *Added in a later pass the same day:* `backend/src/common/errors/error-codes.spec.ts` holds `AUTH_ERROR_CODES` to the web client's `AuthErrorCode` union member for member and in order (it was only a comment before), and pins a codeless 401 to `unauthorized`, which M39.4's refresh rule depends on.

Why the server-backed reveal cannot be wired yet, from `patient.service.ts`: a doctor sees only their own patients and sees them in the clear, so a doctor reveal unlocks nothing; the masked directory and `revealIdentity` serve every other role platform-wide, so wiring an administrator's reveal would grant every admin every hospital's identities, which OD-07 forbids.

## Milestone 41: Mobile API Validation

**Goal:** Prove mobile-readiness (master guide §15).

- [x] **M41.1** — Token-in-body auth variant (`X-Client-Type: mobile`) exercised by a script client; refresh rotation identical.
  - ✅ Verify: script completes login→refresh→logout without cookies.
  - **Verified (status reconciled 2026-10-24) — `test/auth-mobile-client.e2e-spec.ts`, which was written as this script and says so in its header.** Over HTTP with no cookie jar: login returns the refresh token in the body and sets no cookie; refresh rotates through the body and only the rotated token works; replaying a rotated token kills the family (reuse detection is the web path's); logout ends the session with the access token alone; a doctor account behaves the same. The web path is asserted unchanged in the same file — cookie and no body token, no body token leaked on a cookie refresh, an unrecognised `X-Client-Type` ignored, a cookie winning over a body token. **Exactly one copy of the credential is issued per client type**, which is the property the file names as the one that matters. The "script client" is the in-process HTTP client the suites use, not a standalone script; the verify step's property — no cookies, end to end — is what it asserts.
- [ ] **M41.2** — Push-device registration endpoint (`/me/devices` extension) + payload-size audit of list DTOs + ISO-8601/UTC verification + deprecation-header mechanism documented.
  - ✅ Verify: audit report committed; timestamps all UTC in contract tests.
  - **Push-device registration — built 2026-10-30; the box stays unticked for the payload-size audit, the UTC verification and the deprecation-header note.** `POST /me/devices` takes an optional `pushToken`, stored on `trusted_devices.push_token` (migration `20261030140000_trusted_device_push_token`) and never returned. The M8.4 `TrustedDevice` shape is unchanged. One device holds a given token: registering it again moves it, and removing the device clears it. Delivery goes through a new port, `shared/push` (`PUSH_PROVIDER`), bound to `UnconfiguredPushProvider`, so a deployment records `skipped / no-provider` exactly as before. With a provider bound: `skipped / no-device`, `sent` with the provider's reference, `failed / invalid-token` (token cleared) or `failed / send-failed`. `test/push-delivery.e2e-spec.ts` (11, scripted provider). **No real push service is chosen or verified**; FCM, APNs or Expo plus credentials are the owner's, per [`EXTERNAL_INTEGRATIONS.md`](EXTERNAL_INTEGRATIONS.md).
  - **Finding 2026-11-14, not fixed — the owner did not approve the fix (2026-11-14: "Push Delivery production change: Not approved. Keep `NotificationDeliveryService.finish()` unchanged").** `NotificationDeliveryService.finish` writes a claimed row's outcome with `update`, which throws (Prisma P2025) when the row no longer exists. A row can vanish between the claim and the write when its notification is deleted (the delivery cascades). The run then rejects, and **every row claimed after it in that batch stays in `sending`**, a status `claim` never picks up again, so those deliveries are never attempted. The batch's delivery order is unspecified (`claim` orders the claim, not the rows it returns), so which rows strand is arbitrary. Production code deletes neither notifications nor deliveries today, so the path is not reachable as built; it is reached in the test suite by other files' cleanup, and would become reachable with any future retention purge, account erasure or notification delete. Pinned as executable evidence by the last case of `test/push-delivery.e2e-spec.ts` (it asserts today's behaviour and fails when the behaviour changes). Recommended fix, should the owner take it up separately: write the outcome with a conditional `updateMany` and skip a row that is gone, which is the same `UPDATE … WHERE id = $1` for every row that exists. The test suite is isolated from the defect without a production change (`test/support/delivery-worker-lock.ts`).
  - **ISO-8601/UTC — verified 2026-10-30; the box stays unticked for the payload-size audit and the deprecation-header note.** `test/timestamps-utc.e2e-spec.ts` discovers every parameterless `GET` Express serves (83), calls each as admin, doctor, reception, laboratory, pharmacy and patient over HTTP, with a chart and a lab order in `processing` as fixtures, and walks every JSON body. Every string that starts like a date-time must be `YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm:ss[.sss]Z`, and every `…At` field must be such a string or `null`. Last run: 206 bodies, 187 instants, no violation. Floors on route, body and instant counts stop it passing on an empty sweep. A second case pins the rule itself: naive, offset, space-separated, epoch and date-only `…At` values are refused. Date-only values (`dateOfBirth`) and times of day are not instants and are not matched. Opaque `cursor` fields are skipped, because the audit cursor begins with an instant (`<occurredAt>|<id>`). **Not covered:** routes with path parameters (a placeholder id answers 404); they return the same view types as the lists.
  - **Payload-size audit — done 2026-11-14; the box stays unticked for the deprecation-header note alone.** `test/list-payload-size.e2e-spec.ts` sweeps every parameterless `GET` as a doctor, an administrator and a patient, asserts that every paginated list refuses `pageSize=101` (API §0: max 100) and that no JSON body carries credential material by key name, and prints bytes per body and per item. The report is [`PATIENT_MOBILE_ENABLEMENT.md`](PATIENT_MOBILE_ENABLEMENT.md) §7. The first run found one defect, fixed in the same change: `GET /administration/staff` accepted up to 200 (default 50) against §0's 100 (default 20). **Deprecation headers — blocked on an owner decision, not built** (owner, 2026-11-14: "M41.2 deprecation window: Remains open. Do not invent a support duration"): master guide §15.5 names "deprecation headers" but no mechanism exists in the code and none is specified. What is undecided is product policy, not header syntax: how long an old mobile build is supported after a field or route is deprecated (the notice window a `Sunset` date encodes), and whether that window differs for the patient app and staff clients. Documenting a mechanism without that number would document a promise nobody has made.

**🔒 Exit gate M41:** mobile validation report committed.
